A10
Project Particulars
Clauses
110 Project Particulars 
1. Project is located St James Marketplace, London
2. The Project measures 13,436m² office NIA, 26,467m² GEA

120 Two Stage Procurement of JCT Design & Build Contract 
1. The Main Contract Works are being procured through a Two Stage Design & Build process 

comprising: 
1.1. Procurement is based on RIBA Stage 3 level of design information. During the First D&B 

Stage, certain packages will be jointly procured by the preferred Main Contractor and 
Client Team to infirm the agreement of the Contract Sum.

1.2. Contract will be awarded based upon RIBA Stage 4 and 5 level of design information, at 
the end of the second D&B Stage, with novation of the Design Team as depicted in the 
Employer's Requirements.

2. The Contractor shall examine the Design, accept full responsibility for its entirety and complete 
the Detailed Design, etc. and deliver the Works, such that they maintain or exceed the quality, 
performance criteria and design/ visual intent of the Design.

3. The Design will generally be of a descriptive nature, requiring the appointment of suitable 
specialist subcontractors, as necessary, to complete the Detailed Design. Refer to Clause 
A21.140 herein for further details.

4. Specialist subcontractors are to complete the Detailed Design, manufacture, supply and 
installation of fully warranted and guaranteed systems, etc. as described in the Specification. 
The Detailed Design submissions are to be reviewed for performance and compliance within 
the Design by the Employer’s Agent and/or the novated Design Team. Any proposed 
alternatives are to be fully visually compliant and be equal or better, in terms of robustness, 
performance, guarantee, suitability and quality to that specified or implied.

5. The Design may include a limited number of Prescriptive Elements, which are for incorporation 
in the Works, strictly in accordance with the Employer's Requirements and manufacturer's 
recommendations.

Ω End of Section

A21
Definitions and Compliance
Clauses
110 Definitions 
1. Defined Terms: The following definitions appear from time to time throughout the 

Specification. Where reference is made using slightly different terms they are to be interpreted 
as one of the below by the Architect. If unsure, request clarification at time of Tender.

2. Design Stages: Design to be carried out as follows: 
2.1. Stage 3: Design Requirements carried out prior to award of the PCSA.
2.2. Stage 4: Detailed Design carried out by the design team or the Contractor.
2.3. Stage 5: Detailed Design, Technical Design and Working Drawings carried out by the 

Contractor or Specialist Subcontractors during the second D&B procurement stage.
3. Employer's Agent: The company or person representing the Client which may also be referred 

to as Employer's Representative or Employer herein.
4. Specification/ Architectural Specification/ this specification: This Specification, comprising 

sections A to Z inclusive, representing the architectural design requirements, which links the 
products, systems and materials shown on the Design Drawings to the Specification via a 
Material and System Sheet (MSS).

5. Design: All information (drawings, schedules, specifications and other supplemental 
information) forming part of the Employer’s Requirements (the Contract information and the 
Contractor’s Proposals) and agreed for Contract purposes, which sets out Descriptive Design, 
the visual intent, any Prescriptive Elements, performance criteria and minimum quality 
standards to be maintained in the completion of the Detailed Design by the Contractor.

6. D(d)etailed D(d)esign: Working Drawings, schedules, detailed specifications and Technical 
Proposals to complete the Design and confirm the final agreed solution to meet the 
fundamental intrinsic requirements of the Design completed by, or on behalf of the Contractor.

7. Working Drawings: Construction drawings and supporting information produced to an 
appropriate scale, showing final co-ordinated layouts, configuration, details, interfaces and 
finishes of the Works.

8. Technical Proposals: Detailed specifications, calculations, assembly drawings, technical 
submittals, data sheets, etc. provided by a specialist subcontractor confirming the final agreed 
solution to the Design.

9. Technical Submittal(s): Product literature, Calculations, Certificates, Technical Data Sheets 
and manufacturers details supplied to confirm the compliance with the specification. All 
submittals to be provided with an executive summary confirming compliance and/or any 
deviations proposed.

10. Descriptive Design: Design information, performance criteria and Visual Reference that 
describe the visual intent of the Works, parts of the Works or elements within the Works where 
the specialist subcontractors shall be responsible for meeting the visual design intent and the 
requirements of the Specification, including any Visual References, including the performance 
criteria.

11. Visual Reference: Where the Design is of a descriptive nature, proprietary products or 
materials may be identified to indicate the visual intent, appearance aspirations, performance 
requirements, product quality, etc. only of the Design. The Contractor may use the stated 
products, having first satisfied themselves as to their full compliance with the Specification,

Definitions and Compliance

performance criteria, as well as contractual and Statutory requirements. Alternatively, the 
Contractor may submit similar products for consideration as set out in clause A21.120.4.

12. Prescriptive Elements: Named elements of the Works that are proprietary systems, products 
or materials, which are to be incorporated in the Works strictly in accordance with the 
manufacturer's recommendations and the Employer's Requirements. If alternative systems, 
products or materials are proposed, the Contractor shall remain responsible for meeting the 
requirements of the Design and such elements should be treated as Descriptive Design.

13. Review Status: The Contractor (including specialist subcontractor) submissions are to be 
reviewed using the A-B-C and D system as described below. The Contractor shall not instruct 
manufacture or installation until A status is achieved for each element of the Works. For clarity 
the status definitions are: 
13.1. A = Reviewed. No comments.
13.2. B = Reviewed. Incorporate comments and resubmit.
13.3. C = Reviewed. Rejected for reasons noted, rework and resubmit.
13.4. D = Not Reviewed. Not applicable to the architectural design scope. 
13.5. If the Review Status is marked 'A' or 'B' it does not relieve the Contractor/ subcontractor of 

their obligations to comply with the design intent and performance criteria, in any way, 
unless explicitly stated otherwise. Where the submittal is marked 'D' it is incumbent for the 
Contractor to ensure that the correct, qualified and appropriate reviewer is utilised.

14. Samples: Labelled physical products/ materials along with supportive technical information, 
representing elements to be incorporated in the Works, subject to acceptance.

15. Visual Mock-ups: Full size, three dimensional indicative model demonstrating size, orientation, 
shape, etc. manufactured from temporary materials.

16. Wider Mock-ups: As 'Visual Mock-ups' but covering multiple elements and sections of the 
Specification and also to include, where required, elements designed by others, including but 
not limited to the Structural Engineer and Services Engineer. Wider Mock-ups to also be used 
for demonstrating buildability and co-ordination/ interface between different Specialist 
contractor elements. 

17. Prototypes: Full size elements representing the final Detailed Design, incorporating actual 
materials and finishes, but not forming part of the permanent works. Prototypes may be tested 
to confirm compliance with performance requirements.

18. Benchmarks: First section of a pre-identified installation on site for inspection and acceptance.
19. A(a)cceptance/ A(a)pproval/ A(a)pproved: Confirmation that the proposed drawings, 

submittals etc. appear to meet the requirements of the Design and the performance criteria as 
set out in the Specification. It does not relieve the Contractor/subcontractor of their obligations 
to comply with the Specification, relevant Codes and Standards, or any other requirement, 
unless explicitly stated otherwise.

20. Additional Information: Other information necessary to adequately demonstrate Detailed 
Design compliance with the Design, including, but not limited to certification, manufacturers’ 
information, warranties/ guarantees, programme/ delivery information, detailed QA/ QC 
systems, relevant reports and data/ calculations. 

21. Reviewed: The review of a Technical Proposal for non-compliance with the Design.

140 Use of Specialist Subcontractors 
1. The Contractor shall engage specialist subcontractors on a design and build basis where 

stipulated in the Specification, to ensure that the Detailed Design, including Technical 
Proposals comply with the requirements of the Design and provide adequate warranties and 
guarantees. The Design of such elements will be of a descriptive nature, will be completed by 
the Contractor's in-house, outsourced, or novated design team through the production of 
Working Drawings and Specifications or other methods as agreed.

2. Subcontractors will be required to fully comply with the Design. Any variation or alternatives 
must be submitted as technical proposals to demonstrate the alternatives meet or exceed the 
requirements of the Design, only agreed and equivalent or better (technically, performance and 
visual) design alternatives will be acceptable. If alternative designs are put forward, the 
specialist subcontractors shall remain responsible for meeting the requirements of the 
Design, Specification, and the performance criteria, as set out above.

3. All specialist subcontractors are to provide collateral warranties in favour of the Employer, to 
the effect that the specialist subcontractor will have the same obligations to the Employer as 
the Contractor has to the Employer with respect to the specialist subcontractors’ work.

4. The Contractor shall provide a list of proposed specialist subcontractors with the Tender 
return.

5. Specialist subcontractors to be engaged to complete the Detailed Design (including Technical 
Proposals), for the following elements as described in the Design Responsibility Matrix.

141 Architectural Specification Design Type 
1. Specialist subcontractors shall be engaged to complete the Detailed Design (including the 

provision Technical Proposals), in accordance with the specification categories P, P/D or D as 
defined below, for the elements of the works as described in the Design Responsibility Matrix.

2. P - Performance Specification (with Prescriptive elements) and Detailed Design intent.
The Designer/Specifier outlines performance requirements and may reference specific 
products or systems. The specialist contractor can propose equivalent alternatives. Sub-
contractors select proprietary products that meet specifications, providing technical 
submissions for approval and following manufacturer recommendations during installation.

3. P/D - Performance Specification with Descriptive Design intent requiring Contractor 
design input/assistance).
The Designer/Specifier requires technical submissions and proposals to ensure the 
contractor's final solution meets requirements without significant deviation from the original 
intent. The Designer coordinates design details and general arrangements, while the 
subcontractor provides input, selects suitable products, adapts them as needed, and ensures 
compliance with performance and design objectives.

Definitions and Compliance

Page 5 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification

04-09-2026 

4. D - Descriptive Design intent and Performance specification (Visual and Functional 
requirements only with full or complex in context contractor design and warranty).
The Designer/Specifier outlines visual and functional intent but does not provide detailed 
design or production information. The subcontractor is responsible for creating a full design 
proposal, selecting products, and constructing and warranting the work to meet the specified 
requirements.

142 Subcontractor responsibility 
1. Specialist subcontractors shall be engaged to complete the Detailed Design (including the 

provision Technical Proposals), in accordance with the sub-contractor responsibility as defined 
below, for the elements of the works as described in the Design Responsibility Matrix. 
1.1. 1: No design by Contractor.

Sub-Contractor Constructs work in accordance with production information and 
prescriptive specification. No design responsibility. Contractor responsible for materials 
and workmanship only. May obtain product manufacturer's guarantees and warranties to 
Client.

1.2. 2: No "in context" design by Contractor. Limited to product selection.
Sub-Contractors Design input limited to selecting 'off the shelf' products that satisfy 
performance specification. Constructs work using selected components. Contractor 
responsible for materials and workmanship and for design input in selecting products. 
May obtain product manufacturer's guarantees and warranties for Client.

1.3. 3: Simple "in context" design by Contractor. Limited to product selection and adapted 
details.
Specialist Sub-Contractor input limited to selecting 'off the shelf' products that satisfy the 
performance specification and adapting them as necessary to suit the Specifier's design. 
Constructs work using selected components as adapted.
Sub-Contractor responsible for materials and workmanship and for design input in 
selecting and adapting products. May obtain product manufacturer's guarantees and 
warranties for Client.

1.4. 4: Complex "in context" design by Contractor. Provides production information (shop 
drawings).
Specialist Sub-Contractor provides detail design and production information, including 
confirming prescriptive specification of products. Constructs work in accordance with 
that design, using selected components adapted as necessary. Specialist's 
Subcontractor responsible for materials and workmanship and for design compliance with 
design intent. May obtain product manufacturers' guarantees and warranties for Client.

1.5. 5: Full Contractor Design. Complete the full package including drawings and 
specification.
Contractor Completes design for the project and all production information, including 
prescriptive specifications. Constructs complete package in accordance with that design. 
Contractor's responsibilities for design of package and for materials and workmanship. 
May obtain product manufacturer's guarantees and warranties for Client.

have been superseded/ replaced/ enhanced the version published at the time of Contract

2. Generally: All products, materials, components, systems, etc. to be new (unless otherwise

and overall appearance.

measurement is to be cumulative in nature to ensure completeness.

an appropriate sequence.

6.1. Compliance: With BS 8000.

published by the British Council of Offices. Do not incorporate any of the following in the 
Works: 

• Asbestos of any kind.
• Wood wool slabs.
• Lead, lead paint or other liquid applications. (Note: Some roofing and rainwater goods 

containing minimal amounts of lead may be acceptable in certain circumstances. Seek 
acceptance prior to ordering).

• Urea formaldehyde foam and any product that may release formaldehyde.
• Polyurethane based insulation.
• Expanded polystyrene and polyurethane.
• Composite metal panels containing an interstitial polyethelene insulated core.
• Chloroflourocarbons and hydrochloroflourocarbons of any kind.
• High alumina cement.
• Tributyltin (TBT).
• Chromated Copper Arsenate (CCA).
• Pentachlorophenol PCP).
• Contaminated gypsum board.
• Pitch polymer DPC.
• Tropical hardwoods.
• Man made or natural mineral fibres that are 3 microns diameter and 200 microns in 

length, to include those that are encapsulated, unsealed and stabilised preventing 
migration.

• Admixtures containing calcium chloride.
• Polychlorinated biphenyis (PCB) and polychlorinated terphenyis (PCT).
• Lindane wood treatment/ insecticidal spray.

132 Sustainability 
1. Comply with the Employer's sustainability and environmental objectives to achieve the

demonstrating achievement of the target rating score. The Final Assessment is to confirm and 
compare where overarching sustainability criteria have been met, exceeded or not met.

2. Comply with the project Sustainability Report(s) to achieve target ratings and the Crown 
Estates's DSP V4 Performance requirements.

3. Follow the principles of the relevant Standards set out in clause A33.132.1 so that the Detailed 
Design maximises the use of materials, products, systems, techniques, methods etc. which are 
consistent with those Standards and good sustainability practice.

4. With regard material selection and use pay particular attention to the following aspects to 
minimise the detrimental impact of the project on the health and wellbeing of the planet and 
occupants: 
4.1. Environmental Product Declarations (EPD's) including compliance with BS EN ISO 14025, 

BS EN 15804 and ISO 21930. EPDs to be provided for all systems and materials covered 
within this Specification to inform product selection and whole life carbon calculations to 
be checked against the design stage LCA. 

4.1.1. Where EDPs, or an EPD without embodied carbon data is not available, a complete 
EPD should be requested from the manufacture/supplier. 

4.1.2. Supply Health Product Declaration (HDP) for interior finishes and finish materials, 
furnishings, and built-in furniture.

4.2. Durability: All key exposed building elements shall align with the relevant industry 
durability or quality standards as required by BREEAM. 

4.3. Sourcing of materials: All construction materials should be sourced in accordance with 
the project's Sustainable Procurement Plan. Sourcing should be in line with guidance 
provided by BS ISO 20400 and requirements set out in clause A33.131.11.

4.4. Material Efficiency Principles should follow BS8895: Parts 1 to 3.
4.5. Transportation confirmed by a travel plan that minimises delivery miles.
4.6. Waste should be minimised and properly disposed of. Refer to project's Sustainability 

Report for waste targets.
4.7. Prioritise low VOC products, natural materials and minimum standards for product 

selection based on British Standards, and EU standards.
4.8. Recycled Content of materials and/or products: Refer to project's Sustainability Report for 

recycled content targets.
4.9. Circular economy principles including but not limited to, design for adaptability, design for 

construction, circular materials selection and recourse efficiency. Refer to project's 
Sustainability Report for circular economy targets.

4.10. Climate change risk as defined by the IPCC Assessment Reports.

135 Quality of Execution 
1. Generally: Fix, apply, install or lay products securely, accurately, plumb, neatly and in alignment. 
2. All of the Works to be completed to the satisfaction of the Architect.
3. Compliance: Execute the Works to a very high acceptable standard and comply with: 

• BS 8000
• BS EN ISO 8501: Part 1:
• BS 5493:
• BS EN ISO 12944: Parts 1-8:
• BS EN ISO 14713: 

4. Visible elements: Angles, junctions, edges, corners, arises, component interfaces and all visible 
elements to be tight, smooth, correctly positioned, neat, properly aligned, and ready for use 
without remedial work. 

5. Tolerances: Maintain acceptable tolerances at all times. 
6. Preceding works:  

6.1. Check and ensure accuracy of preceding elements before commencement of installation 
to guarantee accuracy. 

6.2. Do not cut, drill or alter the preceding work or interfacing installations without prior 
authorisation. Any such authorised work shall not change the visual intent. 

7. Sequencing: 
7.1. Implement proper sequencing of elements and components to guarantee quality of final 

installation.
7.2. Ensure relevant work areas/building zones are suitably watertight before commencing 

internal finishes. 
8. Preparation: Surfaces to receive treatment or attachments to be suitably prepared to ensure 

accurate installation without causing any damage or reduction in performance, quality or visual 
intent. 

9. Tools: Minimise use of power activated tools. 
10. Cleaning: 

10.1. Blast cleaning: Blast cleaning to be grade Sa 2.5 after removal of all debris and deposits to 
achieve required surface finish quality. Primer to be applied immediately after blast 
cleaning. 

A33
Quality standards/ control

Page 12 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification
Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

10.2. Prior to installation: Remove efflorescence, dirt, dust, traces and any other contaminant 
prior to installation or application of finishes. 

10.3. Handover: Professionally clean the Works prior to handover. 
11. Manufacture: Maximise off site manufacture where specified and appropriate. 
12. Installations: 

12.1. General: Installations to be permanently stable and remain in position without twisting, 
warping, loosening, vibrating, knocking, rattling, whistling, etc. 

12.2. Weather: Do not carry out installations during inclement weather, unless measures have 
been taken to mitigate the effects of low temperature, frost, high rainfall, snow, high 
humidity, etc. 

13. Joints: 
13.1. Joints to be as shown on the Working Drawings, be perfectly formed, tight, properly 

aligned, without lipping and visually acceptable, all within stipulated tolerances.
13.2. Movement joints to be installed as shown on the Working Drawings.
13.3. Minimise the number of joints and maximise alignment. 

14. Fire Stopping: Provide permanent fire stopping solutions maintaining compliance with the visual 
intent. Fire stopping to meet all fire codes and regulations, including spread of flame and 
smoke. Material to be installed strictly in accordance with manufacturers’ recommendations, 
ensuring that all gaps are properly sealed, and remain permanently in place for the Design Life 
of the Building.

15. Membranes: Fix membranes to adequately cleaned surfaces. 
16. Insulation: Install dry sheet insulation using recommended mechanical or bonded methods, 

horizontally, vertically or sloping, as appropriate, without lipping, gaps or uneven joints. Stagger 
joints when installing filter fabric. 

17. Protection: Protect the work at all times, including all sides at junctions, joints, perimeters, etc. 
when installing high performance, reinforced metal or plastic vapour control and air leakage 
barriers. 

18. Welding: When working on site use manual inert gas tungsten arc welding techniques in 
accordance with BS EN 1011. Welding to be smooth, continuous and visually acceptable. 

19. Colour batching: Do not use different colour batches where they can be seen together. 
20. Dimensions: Check on-site dimensions. 
21. Finished work: Not defective, e.g. not damaged, disfigured, dirty, faulty, or out of tolerance. 
22. Location and fixing of products: Adjust joints open to view so they are even and regular.

351 General structural requirements 
1. Take account of Structural Engineer’s documents and specifications, etc. related to loadings, 

tolerances and design and safely accommodate the worst combination of structural conditions, 
including pressure coefficients. 

2. Take account of BS EN 1991: Part 1. 
3. Ensure rigidity of materials and components so that they are not adversely affected by any 

external or internal vibration and safely withstand all forces and loads imposed at all times. 
4. Install and align all parts of the Work to accommodate deviations of any part of the structure. 
5. Safely accommodate all movement and stresses imposed by the structure as well as all dead/ 

live loads imposed by self weight, attachments, environmental conditions, temporary loads 
during construction, without affecting visual intent. 

6. All movement joints to be visually acceptable. 
7. Components, materials, systems and products to accommodate expansion/ contraction when 

conditions range between 5°C and 28°C air temperature, as well as surface differential 
temperature of between five and eighty degrees centigrade solar radiation. 

8. Working Drawings to show methods by which tolerances of elements, including interfaces, are 
accommodated. 

9. Take into account the following:

• BS 6180. 
• BS EN 1991 - Eurocode 1.
• BS EN 1991 Parts 1 - 4 (as applicable to location). 9.4. PD 6688. 

10. All elements to resist all static and dynamic loads, without deformation or changes to visual 
intent, including conditions of extreme wind loads, moving elements/ components in close 
proximity and unexpected water absorption. 

11. Take account of all loads imposed during cleaning and maintenance operations including 
operatives, machinery, products, temporary structures and the like to a minimum of 500 
Newtons applied through 100mm square.

12. All imposed loads to be safely transferred to the adjoining structure, without detrimental effect 
to visual intent, performance or functionality.

13. Accommodate all deflections, including those of a temporary nature, without detrimental effect 
to visual intent, performance or functionality.

14. Maximum allowable deflection, when fully loaded, to be the lower of either 15mm or 1/175 of 
clear span.

15. Accommodate all thermal movements caused by climatic conditions externally and internally. 
Provide necessary permanent/ temporary structural support, as necessary, and take measures 
to mitigate against noise arising.

16. All components, products, materials, fixtures, fittings and systems are to remain rigid under all 
conditions unless purposefully designed otherwise.

17. Maximum permitted deflection to be span/400 (span maximum 1500mm). 

352 General environmental requirements 
1. Take account of Services Engineer’s documents, specifications, Approved Document Part L of 

the Building Regulations, etc. related to local conditions, performance requirements, 
eradication of cold bridging etc. 

2. When selecting materials, components, products, etc. take account of prevailing local 
conditions to ensure suitability for the Design and Service Life of the Works. 

3. All products, materials, components etc. to be moisture and water resistant, including resisting 
changes in moisture content of air or humidity, the effect of freezing and material moisture 
content. 

4. Protect against frost, precipitation of all kinds, humidity, etc. during manufacture, delivery, 
storage and installation up to handover.

5. Maintain weatherproof and watertight barriers between external and internal conditions, 
including prevention of water ingress, condensation and capillarity at all times and under all 
conditions.

6. External, internal or interstitial condensation is not acceptable. Comply with BS 5250 and other 
applicable codes or standards with regard condensation calculation methods and demonstrate 
compliance through calculations, testing and adequate detailing.

8. Weatherproofing/ Watertightness: 
8.1. Comply with the requirements of BS EN 1027.
8.2. Tests of facades to confirm that the greater of class 600 Pa or 0.25 of design wind 

pressure has been achieved in respect of water penetration.
8.3. All external walls/ roofs are to be tested or certified to demonstrate that the building is

immediately notified, repaired and re-tested. 

9. Flood Testing: 
9.1. Supervised and recorded site flood tests to be actioned, as necessary. Such tests are to 

be carried out by filling the designated area with water to a depth of at least 50mm and 
12mm below kerb level, after sealing all outlets, joints, junctions, etc. for a minimum period 
of 48 hours.

9.2. Depths to be checked every four hours and if lower water levels are recorded, 
immediately locate the leak point, carry out a satisfactory repair and re-test until no drop 
in levels is detected. 

10. Hose testing: Site hose tests to comply with CWCT TN41 - “Site Testing for Watertightness” 
and be carried out on benchmarks and designated areas of the final installation as instructed.

subsequent Fire Authorities’ requirements.

E20
Formwork for In-Situ Concrete
Generally/ preparation
100 Compliance 
1. Section to be read in conjunction with the A and Z Sections which form part of the Specification 

and Contract Conditions.
2. Design: Refer to and comply with A10.120 and A21.140.

620 CON-201 Plain Finish To In-Situ Concrete 
1. Description:  'Plain formed' in-situ concrete surface finish, as defined in the National Structural 

Concrete Specification (NSCS) 4th edition.
2. Surfaces:    

2.1. Free from discoloration caused by contamination from release agent, grout leakage, rust 
staining or other sources.

2.2. Smooth even finish with even aggregate distribution and colour consistency.
3. Finish:  Even and dense. 

3.1. Formwork:  Arrange formwork panels in a regular pattern as a feature of the surface.
4. Crack widths:    

4.1. Shall not exceed 0.3 mm generally in the exposed surfaces of finished concrete.
4.2. Water resisting concrete:  Shall not exceed 0.2 mm in the exposed surfaces of finished 

concrete.
5. Permissible deviation of surfaces   

5.1. Sudden irregularities (maximum):  3 mm.
5.2. Gradual irregularities (maximum):  3 mm, when measured from the underside of a 1000 

mm straightedge, placed anywhere on surface.
6. Variations in colour   

6.1. Permitted:  Those caused by impermeable formwork linings.
6.2. Not permitted:  Those caused by contamination or grout leakage.

7. Surface blemishes   
7.1. Permitted:  Blowholes less than 10 mm in diameter and at an agreed frequency.
7.2. Not permitted:  Voids, honeycombing, segregation and other large defects.

8. Formwork tie holes:  In a regular pattern and filled with matching mortar.

Construction
310 Accuracy 
1. General requirement for formwork:  Accurately and robustly constructed to produce finished 

concrete in the required positions and to the required dimensions.
2. Formed surfaces:  Free from twist and bow (other than any required cambers).
3. Intersections, lines and angles:  Square, plumb and true.

E20
Formwork for In-Situ Concrete

Page 26 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification
Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

315 Substructure formwork and underslab insulation 
1. Cutting:  Neat and accurate to edges, and around penetrations and downstands.
2. Laying:  Tightly butted and fully supported on firm, even substrate.
3. Vertical faces:  Stiffen as necessary to act as shutter.
4. Formwork/ insulation surfaces:  Protect from indentation by spacers and other items.
5. Joints in formwork/ insulation and with edge structure and penetrations:  Seal to prevent 

penetration of concrete.
6. Concrete placement:  Restrain formwork/ insulation against movement.

320 Joints in forms 
1. Requirements including joints in form linings and between forms and completed work   

1.1. Prevent loss of grout, using seals where necessary.
1.2. Prevent formation of steps. Secure formwork tight against adjacent concrete.

330 Inserts, holes and chases 
1. Positions and details   

1.1. Dimensioned on drawings provided on behalf of the Employer:  Do not change without 
consent.

1.2. Undimensioned or from other sources:  Submit proposals.
2. Positioning relative to reinforcement:  Give notice of any conflicts well in advance of placing

690 Adverse weather 
1. General:  Do not use frozen materials or lay on frozen surfaces.
2. Air temperature requirements:  Do not lay bricks/ blocks: 

2.1. In cement gauged mortars when at or below 3°C and falling or unless it is at least 1°C and 
rising.

2.2. In hydraulic lime:sand mortars when at or below 5°C and falling or below 3°C and rising, or 
as manufacturer’s/ supplier’s recommendations.

2.3. In thin layer mortars when outside the limits set by the mortar manufacturer.
3. Temperature of walling during curing:  Above freezing until hardened.
4. Newly erected walling:  Protect at all times from: 

4.1. Rain and snow.
4.2. Drying out too rapidly in hot conditions and in drying winds.

4. Finished work:  Free from patches, horizontal stripes and racking back marks.

760 Appearance 
1. Brick/ block selection:  Do not use units with damaged faces or arrises.
2. Cut masonry units:  Where cut faces or edges are exposed cut with table masonry saw.
3. Quality control:  Lay masonry units to match relevant reference panels. 

3.1. Setting out:  To produce satisfactory junctions and joints with built-in features and 
components.

3.2. Coursing:  Evenly spaced using gauge rods.
4. Lifts:  Complete in one operation.
5. Methods of protecting facework:  Submit proposals.

Installation of dpcs/ cavity trays
415 Installation of horizontal dpcs 
1. Placement:  In continuous lengths on full even bed of fresh mortar, with 100 mm laps at joints 

and full laps at angles.
2. Width:  At least full width of leaf unless otherwise specified. Edges of dpc not covered with 

mortar or projecting into cavity.
3. Overlying construction:  Immediately cover with full even bed of mortar to receive next masonry 

course.
4. Overall finished joint thickness:  As close to normal as practicable.

425 Installation of ground-level dpcs 
1. Joint with damp-proof membrane:  Continuous and effectively sealed.

435 Installation of stepped dpcs in external walls 
1. External walls on sloping ground:  Install dpcs not less than 150 mm above adjoining finished 

ground level.

445 Installation of sill dpcs 
1. Form and placement:  In one piece and turned up at back when sill is in contact with inner leaf.

455 Installation of coping/ capping dpcs 
1. Placement:  Bed in one operation to ensure maximum bond between masonry units, mortar and 

dpc.
2. Dpcs crossing cavity:  Provide rigid support to prevent sagging.

465 Sealing of dpcs 
1. Description:  Generally.
2. Overlaps and junctions: Seal with Adhesive recommended by dpc manufacturer.

475 Installation of site-formed cavity trays 
1. Requirements to prevent downward ingress of water   

1.1. Profiles:  To match those shown on drawings. Firmly secured.
1.2. Joint treatment:  Use continuous length wherever possible, otherwise lap at least 100 mm 

and seal to produce a free draining and watertight installation.
1.3. Horizontal cavity trays:  Support using cavity closer.
1.4. Sloping cavity trays:  Prevent sagging.
1.5. Cleanliness:  Free from debris and mortar droppings.

485 Installation of cavity trays over openings and other cavity bridgings 
1. Length:  To extend not less than 150 mm beyond ends of lintels/ bridgings.

495 Installation of gas-resistant dpcs/ cavity trays 
1. Joint treatment:  Use continuous length wherever possible, otherwise lap at least 150 mm and 

seal to form a gas and watertight installation.

275 Slate fixing 
1. Setting out:  Lay slates with an even overall appearance with slightly open (maximum 5 mm) butt 

joints. Align tails.
2. Slate thickness:  Consistent in any one course. Lay with thicker end as tail.
3. Ends of courses:  Use extra wide slates to maintain bond and to ensure that cut slates are as 

large as possible. Do not use slates less than 150 mm wide.
4. Top course:  Head-nail short course to maintain gauge.
5. Fixing:  Centre nail each slate twice through countersunk holes 20-25 mm from side edges. 

5.1. Nails:  Copper clout to BS 1202-2 or aluminium clout to BS 1202-3.
5.2. Nail dimensions:  Determine in accordance with BS 5534 to suit site exposure, withdrawal 

resistance and slate supplier’s recommendations.

290 Mortar bedding/ Pointing 
1. Mortar:  As section Z21, 1:3 cement:sand, with plasticizing admixtures permitted. 

1.1. Bond strength providing resistance to uplift:  In accordance with BS 5534.
2. Weather:  Do not use in wet or frosty conditions or when imminent.
3. Preparation of concrete and clay tile accessories to be bedded:  Wet and drain surface water 

before fixing.
4. Appearance:  Finish neatly as work proceeds and remove residue.

Roof slating edges/ junctions/ features
305 Generally 
1. Fittings and accessories:  As recommended by slate supplier, do not improvise. 

1.1. Exposed fittings and accessories:  To match slate colour and finish.
2. Cut slates:  Cut only where necessary, to give straight, clean edges.
3. Flashings:  Fix with or immediately after slating. Form neatly.

325 Fire-separating walls 
1. Separating walls:  Completely fill space between top of wall and underside of slates with mineral 

wool quilt to provide fire-stopping.
2. Boxed eaves:  Completely seal air paths in plane of separating wall with wire-reinforced mineral 

wool, not less than 50 mm thick, fixed to rafters and carefully cut to shape fire-resisting board 
and quilt to provide fire-stopping.

445 Mortar-bedded verges with bedded undercloak 
1. Underlay:  Carry 50 mm onto outer leaf of gable wall and bed on mortar.
2. Undercloak:  Slates. 

2.1. Position:  Over underlay, level with underside of slating battens, sloping towards verge.
2.2. Projection beyond face of wall:  38-50 mm.
2.3. Bedding:  On mortar identical to that used in gable walling.

3. Slating battens:  Carry onto undercloak and finish 100 mm from verge edge.
4. Verge slates   

4.1. Bedding:  Flush with undercloak on 75 mm wide bed of mortar.
4.2. Pointing:  Flush profile.
4.3. Fixing:  Do not displace or crack mortar.

455 Mortar-bedded verges with nailed undercloak 
1. Underlay:  Carry over full width of verge.
2. Undercloak:  Slates. 

2.1. Position:  Over underlay, level with underside of slating battens, sloping towards verge.

645 Laced valleys 
1. Preparatory work:  Lay general roof underlay and slating battens into valleys.
2. Valley boarding:  Timber 25 mm thick. 

2.1. Centre board:  Minimum 250 mm wide.
2.2. Side boards:  100 mm wide and feather-edged.

3. Underlay:  Lay strips not less than 600 mm wide centred on valleys.
4. Valley slates:  Form intersections of slate courses by laying square slates diagonally on centre 

of valley boarding. Sweep roof slate courses neatly upwards to meet the square slates at right 
angles, maintaining specified lap. Fix to battens or direct to valley boarding.

650 Swept valleys 
1. Preparatory work:  Lay general roof underlay and slating battens into valleys.
2. Valley boarding:  Timber 25 mm thick. 

2.1. Centre board:  Minimum 250 mm wide.
2.2. Side boards:  100 mm wide and feather-edged.

3. Underlay:  Lay strips not less than 600 mm wide centred on valleys.
4. Valley slates:  Cut extra wide and/ or extra long slates to maintain bond and laps, and to give a 

smooth sweep through valleys. Fix to battens or direct to valley boarding.

1. Risk of interstitial condensation in roof construction:  Assess in accordance with BS 5250.
2. Vapour control layer:  If necessary, provide a suitable membrane so that damage and nuisance 

from interstitial condensation do not occur.

230 Thermal performance 
1. Requirement:  Determine type and thickness of insulation and integral or separate overlay to

1.3. Substrate surface:  Suitably even, stable and robust to receive roof coatings.
1.4. Insulation compliance:  To a relevant European Standard, or Agrément-certified.

240 Fire performance 
1. Classification:  Broof(t4) in accordance with BS EN 13501-5

2. Exposed substrate:  Do not damage.

530 Making good existing liquid-applied waterproof roof coatings 
1. General:  Inspect for adherence and repair defective areas in accordance with proposed 

coating manufacturer's recommendations.

535 Making good existing reinforced bitumen membrane covering 
1. Blisters:  Star-cut, dry out and rebond.
2. Cracked and defective areas:  Cut back to substrate.
3. Substrate:  Dry out.
4. Bitumen membrane:  Patch level with existing surface with layers of matching bitumen 

membrane, lapped minimum 100 mm onto existing membrane.

540 Making good existing mastic asphalt covering 
1. Defective areas:  Soften and carefully cut out. 

1.1. Hammers, chisels, etc:  Do not use to cut cold mastic asphalt.
1.2. Substrate:  Dry out.
1.3. Separating membrane:  Make good.
1.4. Mastic asphalt:  Patch level with existing surface in two coats, the topcoat lapped 

minimum 75 mm onto existing mastic asphalt and to half its depth.

545 Making good existing fibre cement sheet covering 
1. Loose and soft surfaces:  Wet and remove. Allow to dry.
2. Cracked and damaged sheets:  Replace.
3. Bolt heads:  Tighten and crop where necessary. 

3.1. Corrosion and oxidation:  Abrade back to bright metal.
4. Asbestos-based materials:  Making good or removal must be carried out by a contractor 

licensed by the Health and Safety Executive prior to commencement of other works in their 
location.

550 Making good existing metal sheet covering 
1. Loose coatings:  Remove.
2. Corrosion and oxidation:  Abrade back to bright metal.
3. Structurally unsound sheets:  Replace.

555 Making good existing cementitious slabs/ screeds 
1. Loose surfaces, sharp edges and projections:  Remove.
2. Hollow surfaces, voids and cracks:  Fill with cement-based repair mortar.

560 Existing edge trims 
1. Fasteners:  Check security. Replace as necessary.
2. Existing coverings:  Cut out from edge trim recess sufficient to accommodate coatings.

565 Existing gutters/ outlets 
1. Dirt, debris and build-up of previous coverings/ coatings:  Remove to restore free flow of water.

recommendations and shall only be used if samples of the joints have been accepted by 
the Employer's Agent.

Installation
435 Dry linings generally 
1. General:  Use fixing, jointing, sealing and finishing materials, components and installation 

methods recommended by board manufacturer.
2. Cutting gypsum boards:  Neatly and accurately without damaging core or tearing paper facing. 

2.1. Cut edges:  Minimize and position at internal angles wherever possible. Mask with bound 
edges of adjacent boards at external corners.

3. Fixings boards:  Securely and firmly to suitably prepared and accurately levelled backgrounds.

4. Finishing:  Neatly to give flush, smooth, flat surfaces free from bowing and abrupt changes of 
level.

5. Wet or Humid conditions: :  Outer layer of gypsum board to be moisture resistant with 
appropriate performance characteristics to suit the system. 

445 Ceilings 
1. Sequence:  Fix boards to ceilings before installing dry lined walls and partitions.
2. Orientation of boards:  Fix with bound edges at right angles to supports and with ends 

staggered in adjacent rows.
3. Two layer boarding:  Stagger joints between layers.

455 Metal framing for partitions/ wall linings 
1. Setting out:  Accurately aligned and plumb. 

1.1. Frame/ Stud positions:  Equal centres to suit specified linings, maintaining sequence 
across openings.

1.2. Additional studs:  To support vertical edges of boards.
2. Fixing centres at perimeters (maximum):  600 mm.
3. Fixings:  Securely back to suitable structure accommodating all deflections.  

4. Openings:  Form and seal accurately with a water based acrylic mastic fire seal without 
detriment to the appearance or performance of the system. 
4.1. Doorsets:  Use sleeved or boxed metal studs and/ or suitable timber framing to achieve 

strength grade requirements for framing assembly and adequately support weight of 
door.

4.2. Services penetrations:  Allow for associated fire-stopping.

465 Staggered stud partitions 
1. Horizontal frame members (noggins, bearers, etc.) and boards:  Fix between alternate studs and 

not touching adjacent offset studs.

475 Metal furrings for wall linings 
1. Setting out:  Accurately aligned and plumb. 

1.1. Vertical furring positions:  Equal vertical centres to suit specified linings, maintaining 
sequence across openings. Position adjacent to angles and openings.

1.2. Additional vertical furrings:  To support vertical edges of boards and at junctions with 
partitions.

1.3. Horizontal furring positions:  To provide continuous support to edges of boards.
2. Adhesive bedding to furrings   

2.1. Dabs:  Length 200 mm (minimum). Located at ends of furrings and thereafter at 450 mm 
(maximum) centres.

2.2. Junctions with partitions:  Continuous bed with no gaps across cavity.

485 Suspended ceiling grids 
1. Setting out:  Accurately aligned and level. 

1.1. Grid members and hangers:  Centres to suit specified linings and imposed loads. 
1.1.1. Centres to suit specified linings and imposed loads.

2.2. Installation:  Form in a continuous line with no gaps to provide a complete barrier to smoke

545 Cavity fire barriers within suspended ceilings

3. Ceiling void subdivision: Fix barriers as drawings.
4. Fixing at perimeters and joints:  Secure, stable and continuous with no gaps, to provide a 

complete barrier to smoke and flame.
5. Service penetrations:  Cut and pack to maintain barrier integrity. Sleeve flexible materials.

6. Ceiling systems for fire protection:  Do not impair fire-resisting performance of ceiling system.

555 Fire-stopping at perimeters of dry lining systems

560 Joints between boards

3. Square edged gypsum fibre boards:  5 mm gap.

565 Vertical joints 
1. Joints:  Centre on studs. 

1.1. Partitions:  Stagger joints on opposite sides of studs.
1.2. Two layer boarding:  Stagger joints between layers.

570 Horizontal joints 
1. Surfaces exposed to view:  Horizontal joints not permitted. Seek instructions where height of 

partition/ lining exceeds maximum available length of board.
2. Two layer boarding:  Stagger joints between layers by at least 600 mm.
3. Edges of boards:  Support using additional framing. 

3.1. Two layer boarding:  Support edges of outer layer.

575 Plank plasterboard 
1. First layer in two layer boarding:  Square edged with long edges at right angles to studs.

580 Insulation backed plasterboard 
1. General:  Do not damage or cut away insulation to accommodate services.
2. Installation at corners:  Carefully cut back insulation or plasterboard as appropriate along edges 

of boards to give a continuous plasterboard face, with no gaps in insulation.

590 Fixing gypsum board to metal framing/ Furrings

1.1. Single layer boarding:  To all framing at 300 mm centres. Reduce to 200 mm centres at 
external angles.

1.2. Multi-layer boarding:  Face layer at 300 mm centres, and previous layers around 
perimeters at 300 mm centres.

2. Ceilings:  230 mm. Reduce to 150 mm at board ends and at lining perimeters.
3. Position of screws from edges of boards (minimum):  10 mm. 

3.1. Screw heads:  Set in a depression. Do not break paper or gypsum core.

Finishing
650 Level of dry lining across joints 
1. Sudden irregularities:  Not permitted.
2. Joint deviations:  Measure from faces of adjacent boards using methods and straightedges 

(450 mm long with feet/ pads) to BS 8212, clause 3.3.5. 
2.1. Tapered edge joints   

2.1.1. Permissible deviation (maximum) across joints when measured with feet resting on 
boards:  3 mm.

2.2. External angles   
2.2.1. Permissible deviation (maximum) for both faces:  4 mm.

2.3. Internal angles   
2.3.1. Permissible deviation (maximum) for both faces:  5 mm.

670 Seamless jointing to gypsum boards 
1. Cut edges of boards:  Lightly sand to remove paper burrs.
2. Filling and taping:  Fill joints, gaps and internal angles with jointing compound and cover with 

continuous lengths of paper tape, fully bedded.
3. Protection of edges/ corners:  Reinforce external angles, stop ends, etc. with specified edge/ 

angle bead.
4. Finishing:  Apply jointing compound. Feather out each application beyond previous application 

to give a flush, smooth, seamless surface.
5. Nail/ screw depressions:  Fill with jointing compound to give a flush surface.
6. Minor imperfections:  Remove by light sanding.

680 Skim coat plaster finish 
1. Plaster type As recommended by board manufacturer. 

1.1. Thickness:  2-3 mm.
2. Joints:  Fill and tape except where coincident with metal beads.
3. Finish:  Tight, matt, smooth surface with no hollows, abrupt changes of level or trowel marks. 

3.1. Tight, matt, smooth surface with no hollows, abrupt changes of level or trowel marks.

695 Installing beads/ Stops 
1. Cutting:  Neatly using mitres at return angles.
2. Fixing:  Securely using longest possible lengths, plumb, square and true to line and level,

3. Finishing:  After joint compounds/ plasters have been applied, remove surplus material while 

still wet from surfaces of beads exposed to view.

725 Repairs to existing gypsum board 
1. Performance of repairs must match original specified performances.
2. Filling small areas with broken cores:  Cut away paper facing, remove loose core material and fill 

with jointing compound. 
2.1. Finish:  Flush, smooth surface suitable for redecoration.

3. Large patch repairs:  Cut out damaged area and form neat hole with rectangular sides. Replace 
with matching gypsum board. 
3.1. Fixing:  Use methods to suit type of dry lining, ensuring full support to all edges of existing 

and new gypsum board.
3.2. Finishing:  Fill joints, tape and apply jointing compound to give a flush, smooth surface 

suitable for redecoration.

435 Installing plasterboard panel partitions 
1. Timber or metal framing inserts:  Size to give a firm press fit into the panel cavity. Use for: 

1.1. Panel joints.
1.2. Floor, wall and ceiling perimeters.
1.3. Support for fixtures and fittings:  Mark positions of inserts on panel surface.
1.4. Lining of openings.

2. Perimeter fixing   
2.1. Preparation:  Fix continuous sole plate to floor at 600 mm centres, and 150 mm maximum 

from ends.
2.2. Floor, wall and ceiling:  Fix framing inserts to substrates at 600 mm centres. Locate panels 

into framing inserts and fix at 300mm centres with screws or 230 mm centres with nails.

436 Additional linings to plasterboard panel partitions 
1. General:  Bond additional linings to partitions with 60 mm-wide x 5 mm-thick continuous bands 

of adhesive at 300 mm centres, and centred on joints. Stagger joints between layers by 
minimum 150 mm.

445 Installing plasterboard laminated partitions

1.1. Floor, wall and ceiling perimeters.
1.2. Lining of openings.
1.3. Corners and angle junctions.

2. Perimeter fixing   
2.1. Floor, wall and ceiling:  Fix timber battens to substrates at 600 mm centres.

3. Plasterboard layers

Page 160 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification

3.1. Bonding together:  Apply adhesive in 60 mm-wide x 5 mm-thick continuous bands at 300 
mm centres, and centred on joints. Stagger joints between layers by minimum 150 mm.

3.3. Additional mechanical fixings:  Provide in accordance with partition manufacturer's 

recommendations to achieve specified fire resistance.
4. Complete partitions:  Protect against movement for minimum four hours.

455 Taped seamless finish to plasterboard partitions 
1. Cut edges of boards:  Lightly sand to remove paper burrs.
2. Filling and taping:  Fill joints, gaps and internal angles with jointing compound and cover with 

continuous lengths of paper tape, fully bedded.
3. Protection of edges/ corners:  Reinforce stop ends, external angles, etc. with edge/ angle bead 

or tape.
4. Finishing:  Apply jointing compound. Feather out each application beyond previous application 

to give a flush, seamless surface.
5. Nail/ screw depressions:  Fill with jointing compound to give a flush surface.
6. Minor imperfections:  Remove by lightly sanding.

470 Perimeter seals 
1. Sealant material:  A type recommended by partition/ panel manufacturer.
2. Application:  Continuously, to clean, dry, dust-free surfaces, leaving no gaps. In accordance 

with the sealant manufacturer's instructions.

475 Fire-stopping 
1. General:  Seal gaps at junctions of partitions with perimeter abutments, cavity barriers, service 

penetrations, etc. to prevent penetration of smoke and flame.
2. Materials:  Non-combustible or intumescent.
3. Fire-stopping:  Cross-reference NBS section P12 for requirements of fire-stopping systems.

480 Glazing for relocatable partitions 
1. Glass standards:  To BS 952 and relevant parts of: 

1.1. BS EN 572 for basic soda lime silicate glass.
1.2. BS EN 1096 for coated glass.
1.3. BS EN 1748 for borosilicate glass.
1.4. BS EN 1863 for heat-strengthened soda lime silicate glass.

303 Installation Tolerances

2. Final surface levels: ±2mm of nominal horizontal plane.

4. Gridline creep: 1mm in 10,000mm.

305 Setting out 
1. General:  Completed ceiling should present, over the whole of its surface exposed to the room 

below, a continuous and even surface, jointed (where applicable) at regular intervals.
2. Infill and access units, integrated services:  Fitted correctly and aligned.
3. Edge/ perimeter infill units size (minimum):  Half standard width or length.
4. Corner infill units size (minimum):  Half standard width and length.
5. Grid:  Position to suit infill unit sizes. Allow for permitted deviations from nominal sizes of infill 

unit.
6. Infill joints and exposed suspension members:  Straight, aligned and parallel to walls, unless 

specified otherwise.
7. Suitability of construction:  Give notice where building elements and features to which the 

ceiling systems relate are not square, straight or level.

310 Bracing 
1. General:  Secure, with additional bracing and stiffening to give a stable ceiling system resistant 

to design loads and pressures.

315 Protection 
1. Loading:  Do not apply loads for which the suspension system is not designed.
2. Ceiling materials:  When necessary, remove and replace correctly using special tools and clean 

gloves, etc. as appropriate.

320 Top fixing 
1. Building structure:  Verify suitability.
2. Structural soffit:    

2.1. Suitability to receive specified fixings:  Evaluate and confirm.
3. Fixing generally:  In accordance with BS EN 13964.
4. Fixing to   

4.1. Concrete:  Drill and insert suitable expanding anchors.
4.2. Aerated concrete:  Fix through from the top of concrete units and provide a system of 

primary support channels.
4.3. Structural steel:  Drill, or use suitable proprietary clips/ adaptors.

K40
Demountable Suspended Ceilings

4.4. Metal roof decking:  Fix to sides of liner tray corrugations.
4.5. Timber:  Fix to side of joists at least 50 mm from bottom edge. If ceiling system is intended 

for fire protection, fix into top third of joists.
4.6. Hollow structural members:  Submit fixing proposals.

325 Installing hangers 
1. Wire hangers:  Straighten and tension before use.
2. Installation:  Install vertical or near vertical, without bends or kinks. Do not allow hangers to 

press against fittings, services, or insulation covering ducts/ pipes.
3. Obstructions:  Where obstructions prevent vertical installation, either brace diagonal hangers 

against lateral movement, or hang ceiling system on an appropriate rigid sub-grid bridging 
across obstructions and supported to prevent lateral movement.

4. Extra hangers:  Provide as necessary to carry additional loads.
5. Fixing   

5.1. Wire hangers:  Tie securely at top with tight bends to loops to prevent vertical movement.
5.2. Angle/ strap hangers:  Do not use rivets for top fixing.

6. Spacings:  As per the system manufacturer's recommendations

330 Installing timber edge battens 
1. Fixing:  Firmly to perimeter wall or other building structure. 

1.1. Fasteners:  As per the system manufacturer's recommendations
1.2. Fixing centres (maximum):  As per the system manufacturer's recommendations

335 Perimeter trims 
1. Jointing:  Neat and accurate, without lipping or twisting. 

1.1. External and internal corners: Mitre joints generally. Overlap joints at internal corners are 
not acceptable.

1.2. Intermediate butt joints:  Minimize. Use longest available lengths of trim. Align adjacent 
lengths.

2. Fixing:  Fix firmly to perimeter wall, edge battens or other building structure. 
2.1. Fasteners:  As per the system manufacturer's recommendations
2.2. Fixing centres:  As per the system manufacturer's recommendations

340 Exposed grids 
1. Grid fixings:  As per the system manufacturer's recommendations
2. Main runners:  Install level. Do not kink or bend hangers. 

2.1. Spliced joints:  Stagger.
2.2. Wire hangers passing through main runners:  Use sharp bends and tightly wrapped loops.
2.3. Angle/ strap hangers:  Do not use rivets for bottom fixing.
2.4. Angular displacement of long axis of one runner in relation to next runner in line with 

it:  Not visually apparent.
3. Cross members supported by main runners or other cross members:  Install perpendicular to 

intersecting runners.
4. Cross tees:  Flat and coplanar with flanges of main runners after panel insertion. 

K40
Demountable Suspended Ceilings

Page 172 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification
Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

4.1. Cross tees over 600 mm long, cut and resting on perimeter trim:  Provide an additional 
hanger.

5. Holding down clips:  Locate to manufacturer's recommendations. 
5.1. Fire-protecting/ resisting ceiling systems:  Use clip type featured in the fire test/ 

assessment.

345 Concealed grids 
1. Grid fixings:  As per the system manufacturer's recommendations
2. Primary support channels:  Install level. Do not kink or bend hangers. 

2.1. Wire hangers wrapped around primary channels:  Twice wrapped. Loops tightly formed.
2.2. Angle/ Strap hangers:  Do not use rivets for bottom fixing.

3. Splines:  Locate between infill units to assist levelling of adjacent units and to resist air 
movement at joints.

4. Spring-tee grids:  Do not omit primary channel.

355 Installing infill units 
1. General   

1.1. Perimeter infill units:  Trimmed, as necessary, to fully fill space between last grid member 
and perimeter trim. Prevent subsequent movement.

1.2. Deeply textured infill units:  Minimize variations in apparent texture and colour. In 
particular, avoid patchiness.

2. Concealed grids:  Install infill units uniformly, straight and aligned. Avoid dimension creep. 
2.1. Infill units around recessed luminaires and similar openings:  Prevent movement and 

displacement.

365 Installing metal infill units 
1. Sound absorbing pads:  Fit to prevent upward air movement through infill units. Cut or fold pads 

in cut perimeter infill units to full unit size. Reseal cut pads.
2. Perimeter infill units:  Firmly wedge cut units into perimeter trim, or clip down.

375 Board ceiling systems 
1. Cut boards:  Neat and accurate.
2. Fixing to grid   

2.1. Board edges:  Fully support. Screw to grid members. Set heads of screws below surface 
of boards and fill flush with surface.

2.2. Boards applied in two or more layers:  Stagger joints.
3. Movement joints:  Provide as appropriate for the area of ceiling system and/ or to coincide with 

movement joints in surrounding structure.

385 Upstands and bulkheads 
1. Vertical ceiling systems:  Support and brace to provide alignment and stability.
2. High upstands:  Provide support at base of upstand.

390 Openings in ceiling materials 
1. General:  Neat and accurate. To suit sizes and edge details of fittings. Do not distort ceiling

395 Integrated services 
1. General:  Position services accurately, support adequately. Align and level in relation to the 

ceiling and suspension system. Do not diminish performance of ceiling system.
2. Small fittings:  Support with rigid backing boards or other suitable means. Do not damage or 

distort the ceiling. 
2.1. Surface spread of flame rating of additional supporting material:  Not less than ceiling 

material.
3. Services outlets   

3.1. Supported by ceiling system:  Provide additional hangers.
3.2. Independently supported:  Provide flanges to support ceiling system.

401 Ceiling-mounted luminaires 
1. Support:    

1.1. Independently supported luminaires:  Suspension adjusted to line and level of ceiling.
1.2. Ceiling supported luminaires: Modifications and/ or extra support required: To each 

luminaire.
2. Surface mounted luminaires:  Units installed so that in event of a fire the designed grid 

expansion provision is not affected.
3. Modular fluorescent recessed luminaires:  Compatible with ceiling module. Extension boxes 

must not foul ceiling system.
4. Recessed rows of luminaires:  Provide flanges for support of grid and infill units, unless 

mounted above grid flanges. Retain in position with lateral restraint.
5. Fire-protecting/ resisting ceiling systems:  Luminaires must not diminish protection integrity of 

ceiling system.
6. Access:  Provide access for maintenance of luminaires.

406 Trunking 
1. Recessed trunking:  Provide flanges for support of grid and infill units, unless mounted above 

grid flanges. Retain in position with lateral restraint.

411 Mechanical services 
1. Fan coil units   

1.1. Inlet/ Outlet grilles:  Trim ceiling grid and infill units to suit.
1.2. Space beneath:  Sufficient for ceiling system components.
1.3. Suspension and connections:  Permit accurate setting out and levelling of fan coil units.

2. Air grilles and diffusers   
2.1. Setting out:  Accurate and level.
2.2. Linear air diffusers:  Retain in place with lateral restraint. Provide flanges for support of 

grid and infill units.
2.3. Grille/ Diffuser ceiling joints:  Provide smudge rings and edge seals.

3. Smoke detectors and PA speakers

1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification

04-09-2026 

3.1. Ceiling infill units:  Scribe and trim to suit.
3.2. Independent suspension:  Required
3.3. Flexible connections:  Required.

4. Sprinkler heads:  Carefully set out and level.

415 Installing insulation 
1. Fitting:  Fit accurately and firmly with butted joints and no gaps.
2. Insulation within individual infill units:  Fit closely. Secure to prevent displacement when infill 

units are installed or subsequently lifted. 
2.1. Dustproof sleeving:  Reseal, if cut.

3. Width:  Lay insulation in the widest practical widths to suit grid member spacings.
4. Services:  Do not cover electrical cables that have not been sized accordingly. Cut insulation 

carefully around electrical fittings, etc. Do not lay insulation over luminaires.
5. Sloping and vertical areas of ceiling system:  Fasten insulation, to prevent displacement.

421 Ceiling systems intended for fire protection 
1. Junctions of ceiling systems with perimeter abutments and service penetrations:  Seal gaps 

with tightly packed mineral wool or intumescent sealant to prevent penetration of smoke and 
flame.

2. Ceiling system/ wall junctions:  Maintain protective value of ceiling system. 
2.1. Fixings and grounds:  Noncombustible.
2.2. Metal trim:  Provide for thermal expansion.

3. Access and access panels:  Maintain continuity of fire protection.

425 Installing cavity fire barriers 
1. Maximum ceiling void dimension in any direction:  To comply with Approved Document

B, volume 1, or volume 2.
2. Fixing:    

2.1. General:  Fix barriers securely to channels or angles at abutments to building structure.
2.2. At perimeters and joints:  Provide permanent stability and continuity with no gaps to form 

a complete barrier to smoke and flame.
3. Joints:  Form to preserve integrity in fire.
4. Service penetrations:  Cut barriers neatly to accommodate services. Fit fire-resistant sleeves 

around flexible materials. Fill gaps around services to fire barrier manufacturer's 
recommendations to maintain barrier integrity. Adequately support services passing through 
the barrier.

5. Ceiling systems intended for fire protection:  Do not impair fire-resisting performance of ceiling 
system.

6. Ceiling systems not intended for fire protection:  Do not mechanically interlink barriers with 
ceiling system.

431 Installing sound barriers 
1. Setting out:  Align accurately with partition heads.
2. Fixing:  Fix tightly at perimeters and joints using methods recommended by barrier 

manufacturer, including steel support sections as appropriate. Completed installation to be 
stable, secure and continuous, with no gaps.

635 Fabrication Tolerances 
1. Squareness:  ±1:1000 deviation to longest side.
2. Length:  ±2mm.
3. Width:  ±2mm.
4. Flatness:  ±1mm under a 600mm straightedge.
5. Twist / Warp:  < 2mm. 

640 Installation generally 
1. Fasteners and methods of fixing:  To Section Z20.
2. Structural members:  Do not modify, cut, notch or make holes in structural members, except as 

indicated on drawings.
3. Temporary support:  Do not use stairs, walkways or balustrades as temporary support or 

strutting for other work.
4. Applied finishes:  Substrates to be even, dry, sound and free from contaminants. Make good 

substrate surfaces and prepare/ prime as finish manufacturer’s recommendation before 
application.

5. Visible fixings: :  Stainless steel hex socket machine (Allen) screws, countersunk where 
possible.

6. Tolerances:    
6.1. Squareness:  No diagonal panel deviation of ±3mm or ±0.075%., whichever is the lowest.
6.2. Levelness:  ±2mm from 2m straight edge
6.3. Flatness:  ±2mm from 2m straight edge
6.4. Vertical joints:  < 1.5mm from another joint.
6.5. Vertical plane:  <1 in 1000. Theoretical position ±1.5mm.
6.6. Bow:  3mm or 0.075% whichever is the lowest.
6.7. Twist:  <1° from either end. 
6.8. Alignment:  ±2mm of other elements. 

670 Installation of tread inserts/ nosings 
1. Treads:  Fully cured, sound and level.

screed.

2. Separating layer   
2.1. Type:  Polyethylene sheet minimum 125 micrometres thick (500 gauge).
2.2. Installation:  Lay over insulation and turn up at perimeter abutments. Lap 100 mm at joints.

100 mm at joints and seal with tape.
2.3. Perimeter:  Maintain isolation of screed.

Finishing/curing
510 Finishing generally 
1. Timing:  Carry out all finishing operations at optimum times in relation to setting and hardening 

of screed material.
2. Prohibited treatments to screed surfaces   

2.1. Wetting to assist surface working.
2.2. Sprinkling cement.

520 Wood floated finish 
1. Finish:  Slightly coarse, even texture with no ridges or steps.

530 Smooth floated finish 
1. Finish:  Even texture with no ridges or steps.

540 Trowelled finish to levelling screeds 
1. Floating:  To an even texture with no ridges or steps.
2. Trowelling:  To a uniform, smooth but not polished surface, free from trowel marks and other 

blemishes, and suitable to receive specified flooring material.

550 Trowelled finish to wearing screeds 
1. Floating:  To an even texture with no ridges or steps.
2. Trowelling:  Successively trowel at intervals, applying sufficient pressure to close surface and 

give a uniform smooth finish free from trowel marks and other blemishes.

560 Dewatered trowelled finish to wearing screeds 
1. Dewatering:  Immediately after compaction of wearing screeds, remove water using a vacuum 

process.
2. Floating:  Without delay, power float to an even texture with no ridges or steps.
3. Trowelling:  Successively trowel at intervals, applying sufficient pressure to close surface and 

give a uniform smooth finish free from trowel marks and other blemishes.

600 Power ground finish to wearing screeds 
1. Floating:  To an even surface with no ridges or steps. Immediately commence curing.
2. Grinding:  When concrete is sufficiently hard for sand particles not to be torn from surface, 

remove 1 - 2 mm from surface to give an even glass-paper texture, free from blemishes and 
trowel marks.

3. Cleaning:  Remove dust and wash down. Resume curing without delay.

M10
Cement Based Levelling/ Wearing Screeds

Page 247 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification
Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

650 Curing 
1. General:  Prevent premature drying. Immediately after laying, protect surface from wind, 

draughts and strong sunlight. As soon as screed has set sufficiently, closely cover with 
polyethylene sheeting.

2. Curing period (minimum): Keep polyethylene sheeting in position for: period recommended by 
screed manufacturer.

3. Drying after curing:  Allow screeds to dry gradually. Do not subject screeds to artificial drying 
conditions that will cause cracking or other shrinkage related problems.

670 Roof screeds 
1. Protection:  Cover screeds during wet weather. When weathertight coverings are laid, screeds 

must be as dry as practicable.

680 Surface sealer to wearing screeds 
1. Manufacturer:  Submit proposals 

1.1. Product reference:  Submit proposals
2. Preparation:  Clean cured screed surface to remove dirt, grease, oil and other surface 

contaminants.
3. Moisture content of screed:  As recommended by sealer manufacturer. Test relative humidity in 

accordance with BS 8203, Annex A if required to verify suitability to receive sealer.
4. Application:  Evenly to dry surfaces using sufficient coats to form an effective seal but without a 

glossy finish.

690 Slip resistance testing of wearing screeds 
1. Test   

1.1. To BS 7976-1, -2 and -3 using a TRL Pendulum.
1.2. When testing with a surface roughness meter, achieve minimum of 20 μm Rz.
1.3. Make arrangements for test to be witnessed/ certified by:  Consultant

2. Report:  Submit. Include slip resistance values in the wet and dry states.
3. Pedestrian screed requirements::  36PTV (wet and dry conditions).

700 Abrasion testing of wearing screeds 
1. Test method:  To BS EN 13892-4.

710 Impact testing 
1. Use 4kg weight, to comply with the BRE Information Paper 11/84 - 'BRE screed tester: 

classification of screeds, sampling and acceptance limits'.

1. Surface finish:  Brushed, with no laitance.
2. Surface preparation:  Suitable to achieve a full bond with bedding. Select from: 

2.1. Keep well wetted for several hours. Remove free water then brush in a slurry bonding coat. 
2.1.1. Slurry:  SBR polymer-modified cement

2.2. Prepare, prime as necessary and apply a bonding agent.

5. Accessories:  None

Fixing
510 Fixing generally 
1. Colour/ shade:  Unintended variations within tiles for use in each area/ room are not permitted. 

1.1. Variegated tiles:  Mix thoroughly.
2. Adhesive:  Compatible with background/ base. Prime if recommended by adhesive 

manufacturer.
3. Use of admixtures with cementitious adhesives:  Only admixtures approved by adhesive 

manufacturer.
4. Cut tiles:  Neat and accurate.
5. Fixing:  Provide adhesion over entire background/ base and tile backs.
6. Final appearance:  Before bedding material sets, make adjustments necessary to give true, 

regular appearance to tiles and joints when viewed under final lighting conditions.
7. Surplus bedding material:  Clean from joints and face of tiles without disturbing tiles.

530 Setting out 
1. Joints:  True to line, continuous and without steps. 

1.1. Joints on walls:  Horizontal, vertical and aligned round corners.
1.2. Joints in floors:  Parallel to the main axis of the space or specified features.

2. Cut tiles:  Minimize number, maximize size and locate unobtrusively.
3. Joints in adjoining floors and walls:  Align.
4. Joints in adjoining floors and skirtings:  Align.
5. Movement joints:  Where locations are not indicated, submit proposals.

540 Level of floor tiling 
1. Permissible deviation in level from datum To be confirmed.

550 Flatness/ Regularity of tiling/ mosaics 
1. Sudden irregularities:  Not permitted.
2. Deviation of surface:  Measure from underside of a 2 m straightedge with 3 mm-thick feet 

placed anywhere on surface. The straightedge should not be obstructed by the tiles and no gap 
should be greater than 6 mm, i.e. a tolerance of +/- 3 mm.

560 Level of tiling across joints 
1. Deviation (maximum) between tile surfaces either side of any type of joint   

1.1. 1 mm for joints less than 6 mm wide.
1.2. 2 mm for joints 6 mm or greater in width.

650 Adhesive bed – notched trowel method (walls) 
1. Application:  By 3 mm floated coat of adhesive to dry background in areas of approximately 1 

m². Comb surface.
2. Tiling:  Press tiles firmly onto float coat.

651 Adhesive bed – notched trowel and buttering method (walls) 
1. Application:  By floated coat of adhesive to dry background in areas of about 1 m². Comb 

surface.
2. Tiling:  Apply thin even coat of adhesive to backs of dry tiles. Fill any ribbed, deep keyed or 

button profiles. Press tiles firmly onto float coat.
3. Finished adhesive thickness:  3 mm or within the range allowed by the adhesive manufacturer.

652 Adhesive bed – buttering method (walls) 
1. Tiling:  Apply even coat of adhesive to backs of dry tiles. Fill any ribbed, deep keyed or button 

profiles.
2. Finished adhesive thickness:  3 mm or within the range allowed by the adhesive manufacturer.

660 Adhesive bed – notched trowel method for mesh-backed mosaic (walls) 
1. Application:  By 3 mm floated coat of adhesive to dry background. Comb surface.
2. Placing mosaic sheets:  Hang in horizontal rows, working downwards. Stagger vertical joints. 

Prevent slippage of sheets. Lightly beat mosaics into adhesive.
3. Width, plane and alignment of joints between sheets:  To match joints between mosaic tiles.

661 Adhesive bed – notched trowel method for paper-faced mosaic (walls) 
1. Application:  By 3 mm floated coat of adhesive to dry background. Comb surface.
2. Preparing mosaic sheets:  Pre-grout. Remove surplus before fixing.
3. Placing mosaic sheets:  Hang in horizontal rows, working downwards. Stagger vertical joints.
4. Width, plane and alignment of joints between sheets:  To match joints between mosaic tiles.
5. Paper face:  Before adhesive hardens completely, remove paper face. Complete grouting. Wash 

off glue from face of mosaic.

690 Cement:sand mortar bedding (walls) 
1. Preparation:  Dampen background.
2. Application:  By floated coat to background: 1:3–4 cement:sand mortar bedding. 

2.1. Thickness (maximum):  10 mm.
2.2. Finish:  Equivalent to wood float. Before tiling allow to stiffen slightly.

3. Tiling:  Without delay, apply 2 mm-thick coat of 1:1 cement:fine sand mortar to backs of tiles, 
filling keys. Press tiles firmly onto float coat. Tap firmly into position.

710 Adhesive bed – notched trowel and buttering method (floors) 
1. Application:  Floated coat of adhesive to dry base and comb surface.
2. Tiling:  Apply coat of adhesive to backs of dry tiles. Fill any ribbed, deep keyed or button 

profiles. Press tiles firmly onto float coat.
3. Finished adhesive thickness:  Within range allowed by manufacturer.

711 Adhesive bed – buttering method (floors) 
1. Tiling:  Apply even coat of adhesive to backs of dry tiles. Fill any ribbed, deep keyed or button 

profiles.
2. Finished adhesive thickness:  Within the range allowed by the adhesive manufacturer.

720 Cement:sand mortar bedding (floors)

2. Laying:  Lay suitably small working areas of screeded bed. Compact thoroughly to level. 

2.1. Finished bed thickness   
2.1.1. For tiles up to 10 mm thick:  10–15 mm.

Completion
820 Finishing 
1. Cleaning:    

1.1. Wash floor with water containing neutral (pH 6–9) detergent. If necessary, lightly scrub 
heavily soiled areas.

1.2. Rinse with clean water, removing surplus to prevent damage to adhesive. Allow to dry.
2. Treatment:  Two coats of a type of emulsion polish recommended by covering manufacturer.
3. Temporary protection:  Cover all trafficked areas with a suitable carpet protection film.

825 Finishing non-textile antistatic flooring 
1. Cleaning operations   

1.1. Wash floor with water containing neutral (pH 6-9) detergent. If necessary, lightly scrub 
heavily soiled areas.

1.2. Rinse with clean water, removing surplus to prevent damage to adhesive. Allow to dry for 
not less than 24 hours before testing.

830 Finishing rubber flooring 
1. Cleaning:  As per manufacturer's recommendations. 
2. Final treatment:  As per manufacturer's recommendations.

2. Polishing:  As per manufacturer's recommendations.

original finish.

original finish.

Preparation
400 Preparation generally 
1. Standard:  In accordance with BS 6150, BS 8000: Part 12, BS EN ISO 2808
2. Refer to any pre-existing CDM Health and Safety File.
3. Refer to CDM Construction Phase Plan where applicable.
4. Suspected existing hazardous materials:  Prepare risk assessments and method statements 

covering operations, disposal of waste, containment and reoccupation, and obtain approval 
before commencing work.

5. Preparation materials:  Types recommended by their manufacturers and the coating 
manufacturer for the situation and surfaces being prepared.

6. Substrates:  Sufficiently dry in depth to suit coating.
7. Efflorescence salts:  Remove.
8. Dirt, grease and oil:  Remove. Give notice if contamination of surfaces/ substrates has occurred.
9. Surface irregularities:  Remove.
10. Joints, cracks, holes and other depressions:  Fill flush with surface, to provide smooth finish.
11. Dust, particles and residues from preparation:  Remove and dispose of safely.
12. Water based stoppers and fillers   

12.1. Apply before priming unless recommended otherwise by manufacturer.
12.2. If applied after priming:  Patch prime.

13. Oil based stoppers and fillers:  Apply after priming.
14. Doors, opening windows and other moving parts   

14.1. Ease, if necessary, before coating.

440 Previously coated surfaces generally 
1. Preparation:  In accordance with BS 6150, clause 11.5.
2. Contaminated or hazardous surfaces:  Give notice of: 

2.1. Coatings suspected of containing lead.
2.2. Substrates suspected of containing asbestos or other hazardous materials.
2.3. Significant rot, corrosion or other degradation of substrates.

3. Suspected existing hazardous materials:  Prepare risk assessments and method statements 
covering operations, disposal of waste, containment and reoccupation, and obtain approval 
before commencing work.

4. Removing coatings:  Do not damage substrate and adjacent surfaces or adversely affect 
subsequent coatings.

5. Loose, flaking or otherwise defective areas:  Carefully remove to a firm edge.
6. Alkali affected coatings:  Completely remove.
7. Retained coatings   

7.1. Thoroughly clean to remove dirt, grease and contaminants.
7.2. Gloss-coated surfaces:  Provide key.

8. Partly removed coatings   
8.1. Additional preparatory coats:  Apply to restore original coating thicknesses.
8.2. Junctions:  Provide flush surface.

9. Completely stripped surfaces:  Prepare as for uncoated surfaces.

2. Dust and nuisance:  Minimize.

Application
711 Coating generally 
1. Application standard:  In accordance with BS 6150, clause 9.
2. Conditions:  Maintain suitable temperature, humidity and air quality during application and 

drying.
3. Surfaces:  Clean and dry at time of application.
4. Thinning and intermixing of coatings:  Not permitted unless recommended by manufacturer and 

agreed with the Architect.
5. Overpainting:  Do not paint over intumescent strips or silicone mastics.
6. Priming coats   

6.1. Thickness:  To suit surface porosity.
6.2. Application:  As soon as possible on same day as preparation is completed.

7. Finish   
7.1. Even, smooth and of uniform colour.
7.2. Free from brush marks, sags, runs and other defects.
7.3. Cut in neatly.

8. Doors, opening windows and other moving parts:  Ease before coating and between coats.
9. Application::  Material from a single batch, providing an even finish, free from any defects. 
10. Thickness::  Paint thickness must always be consistent and checked with a wet film thickness 

wheel or comb gauge. 
11. Drying::  Must never be accelerated through artificial means such as heaters or dehumidifiers. 
12. Final Coat::  Applied as a single coat and once dried it shall be viewed from 2 metres to 

determine colour consistency and quality.
