Intumescent Coatings for Fire Protection of Steelwork

100 Design Compliance 
1. Section to be read in conjunction with the A and Z Sections which form part of the Specification 

and Contract Conditions.
2. Design: Refer to and comply with A10.120 and A21.140.

160 FPS-201 Intumescent Coating Systems 
1. Description:  Factory-applied two pack fast curing epoxy zinc phosphate, intumescent, 

recoatable polyurethane compliant coating.
2. Manufacturer:  AkzoNobel, or acceptable equivalent
3. Use/ location:  Internal exposed steelwork plus internal non-exposed steelwork.
4. Surface:  Blast cleaned structural steelwork.
5. Prmer:    

5.1. Product reference:  Intercure 200.
5.2. Dry film thickness:  75 microns nominal as required for section size, to be determined by 

the Sub-contractor to the acceptance of the Contractor and Structural Engineer.
5.3. C1/C2 environment:  For a C1/C2 environment to BS EN ISO 12944 the Intercure 200 can 

be replaced with Intergard 269 at a nominal dry film thickness of 40 microns. If the 
intumescent is to be Site applied, then shop apply Intercure 200 at 75 microns nominal 
dry film thickness, regardless of environment.

6. Intumescent coat:    
6.1. Product reference:  Interchar 2060.
6.2. Dry film thickness:  As required for section size, to be determined by the Sub-contractor 

to the acceptance of the Contractor and Structural Engineer.
6.3. Fire resistance:  60 minutes/ 90 minutes, to BS 476: Parts 20 and 22, Refer to the Fire 

Strategy Report
7. Topseal:    

7.1. Product reference:  Interthane 990.
7.2. Dry film thickness:  50 microns nominal - two coats required for external exposed 

steelwork, as required for section size, to be determined by the Sub- contractor to the 
acceptance of the Contractor and Structural Engineer

7.3. Colour:  As required by the Contractor
7.4. Finish:  Decorative
7.5. Site touch up:  Repair any intumescent paint damaged during transit or during erection, to 

the manufacturer's working procedures.
7.6. Bolt caps:  Interchar bolt caps to be snapped into place over all structural steel bolt 

connections.
7.7. Refer to manufacturer's working procedures for handling, transportation and storage of 

steelwork treated with Interchar 2060 off Site.

315 New steel – blast-cleaning 
1. Preparation:  Remove oil, grease and contaminants.
2. Blast-cleaning   

2.1. Atmospheric condition:  Dry.
2.2. Abrasive:  Suitable type and size, free from fines, moisture and oil.
2.3. Finish:  To BS EN ISO 8501-1, preparation grade SA2½, with an average profile of 

approximately 75 micrometres.
2.4. Abrasive residues and moisture:  Remove.

3. Primer:  Apply as soon as possible after cleaning and before gingering or blackening appears.

320 Existing steel – blast-cleaning 
1. Preparation:  Remove oil, grease and contaminants.
2. Blast-cleaning:  Remove existing coatings and rust. 

2.1. Atmospheric condition:  Dry.
2.2. Abrasive:  Suitable type and size, free from fines, moisture and oil.
2.3. Finish:  To BS EN ISO 8501-1, preparation grade SA2½, with an average profile of 

approximately 75 micrometres.
2.4. Abrasive residues and moisture:  Remove.

3. Primer:  Apply as soon as possible after cleaning and before gingering or blackening appears.

330 Existing steel – manual cleaning 
1. Preparation:  Remove oil, grease and contaminants.
2. Loose or unsound coatings:  Remove to a firm edge.
3. Finish:  To BS EN ISO 8501-1, preparation grade St2. Leave a clean but unpolished dry surface.
4. Primer:  Apply as soon as possible after cleaning and before gingering or blackening appears. 

Remove coating edges that lift as a result of priming, and reprime.

340 Existing steel – preparation for overcoating 
1. Preparation:  Remove oil, grease and contaminants.
2. Loose or unsound coatings:  Remove to a firm edge.
3. Exposed steel finish:  Manually clean to BS EN ISO 8501-1, grade St2. Leave a clean but 

unpolished dry surface.
4. Existing coatings finish:  Abrade to give a good key. Leave a clean, dry surface.
5. Primer:  Apply one brush coat to bare steel areas. Remove coating edges that lift as a result of 

priming, and reprime.

Application of castings
402 Intumescent casting thickness 
1. Required thickness:  Determine for every steel member to give specified period of fire 

resistance. Use intumescent casting manufacturer's current published loading tables. 
1.1. Special sections and partial fire exposure conditions:  Obtain required thickness in writing 

from manufacturer.
2. Schedule and drawings:  Submit at least two weeks before starting work. 

2.1. Schedule content:  Member sizes, weights/ thicknesses, loading conditions, etc. showing, 
for each variant, the exposed perimeter/ sectional area (Hp/A) ratio and required casting 
thickness.

2.2. Drawing content:  Steelwork drawings marked in colour to show required thickness for 
each member.

404 Intumescent casting installation 
1. Assembly:  Adhesive fix casting sections tight to steel substrate.
2. Filling:  Apply intumescent filler to all joints and deformations to produce a smooth and uniform 

finish.

Application of coatings
410 Intumescent coating dry film thickness (dft) 
1. Required dft:  Determine for every steel member to give specified period of fire resistance. Use 

intumescent coating manufacturer's current published loading tables. 
1.1. Special sections and partial fire exposure conditions:  Obtain required dft in writing from 

manufacturer.
2. Schedule and drawings:  Submit at least two weeks before starting work. 

2.1. Schedule content:  Member sizes, weights/ thicknesses, loading conditions, etc. showing, 
for each variant, the exposed perimeter/ sectional area (Hp/A) ratio and required dft.

2.2. Drawing content:  Steelwork drawings marked in colour to show required dft for each 
member.

420 Measurement of intumescent dft 
1. Primer dft:  Determine average dft (for deduction from total dft after application of 

intumescent).
2. Intumescent dft:  Determine at:

2.1. 500 mm centres along each coated plane of universal sections (8 planes), and rectangular 
hollow sections (4 planes).

2.2. 125 mm centres along coated circular hollow sections, spread evenly around 
circumference.

3. Acceptance standard   
3.1. Average intumescent dft:  Not less than required dft (exclusive of primer and top sealer).
3.2. Local intumescent dft:  Not less than 80% of required dft. Areas greater than 100 mm 

equivalent diameter with a dft of less than 80% of required dft must be brought up to 
thickness.

1. Definition:  High standard of evenness, smoothness and gloss when viewed from a minimum 

distance of 2 m.

490 Top sealer coat

605 Identification and registration labels for suspended access system 
1. Provision   

1.1. Suspension rig and any non-dedicated track systems:  Provide and fix a permanent label 
giving: 

1.1.1. a) Manufacturer’s name, address and telephone number.
1.1.2. b) Name and/ or reference code of model.
1.1.3. c) Serial number and year of manufacture.
1.1.4. d) Rated load of suspended platform (where the platform is permanently attached to 

the system) or maximum total suspended load (where the platform can be 
derigged).

1.2. Suspended platform:  As a), b) and c) above, plus 
1.2.1. d) Rated load and self weight in kg of the platform.
1.2.2. e) Maximum number of persons allowed on the platform.
1.2.3. f) Identification mark or number of the suspension rig or rigs with which the platform 

is compatible (where the platform can be derigged).
1.3. Suspension and safety ropes (detachable):  Marked with length and unique identification 

number.
2. Location:  In positions such that labels can be easily read.

1.4. Maximum load (in kilograms) that may be sustained by the equipment.

2. Location:  In positions such that labels can be easily read.

710 Electrical installation 
1. General:  Install, test and commission electrical work forming part of the access equipment 

system up to and including wiring of isolating switch or switches provided by the electrical 
contractor, all in accordance with BS 7671.

2. Components/ Fasteners:  Use waterproof components and corrosion resisting fasteners 
throughout. Avoid contact between dissimilar metals.

3. Control system:  Designed so that an electrical supply failure, or the failure or malfunction of 
any electrical component, will cause the system to fail to safety.

4. Failsafe provision:  In the event of power failure the system must be capable of delivering the 
working platform to a position of safety either by hand winding or by a controlled gravity 
descent device.

5. Isolating switches:  Clearly labelled to identify purpose. Labels to display an emergency contact 
telephone number.

equipment for the period stated below as and at intervals recommended by the manufacturer.

equipment in an acceptable and safe condition.

2. Service/ Maintenance period:  12 months

640 Installing boarding 
1. Position of boarding:  Across face to full width of opening.
2. Framing:  Provide framing to all edges of boarding and intermediate studs and rails at centres 

not exceeding 450 mm.
3. Bedding:  Bed boarding on fire-resisting adhesive/ sealant.
4. Multiple board layers:  Stagger joints between layers. 

4.1. Joints:  Seal with board adhesive
5. Fixing:  Suitable fixings in accordance with the manufacturers details and recommendations to 

achieve the performance requirements and to suit the service conditions.
6. Other requirements:  Countersink screw heads to and fill and finish joint sealant flush with 

boards

650 Installing flexible intumescent gap sealer 
1. Fitting of strips:  Compress strips and fit into gap so that, as they decompress, the strips wedge 

themselves in the void.
2. Shrink-wrapping:  Do not remove.
3. Joints   

3.1. Ends of strips:  Fit intumescent 'end piece' at both ends of the run of the firestop laminate.
3.2. Joints between strips:  Fit two intumescent 'end pieces' at each butt joint.

660 Applying intumescent foam 
1. New joints:  Remove builders' debris, mortar droppings, grease and other contaminants.
2. Old joints:  Clean and remove existing sealant from each joint.
3. Priming:  Lightly moisten substrate with water.
4. Application:  Fill joint to approximately half its depth, and allow foam to expand to face of joint.
5. Trimming:  Trim excess foam to give a neat, flush appearance

670 Applying intumescent mortar 
1. Sequence:  Install mortar after services are permanently installed.
2. Loose dust and combustible materials:  Remove from the opening, along with any other debris.
3. Shuttering:  Install suitable shuttering panels to the faces of the opening.
4. Temperature:  Do not apply mortar when it could be damaged by frost.
5. Mortar cure:  Do not disturb mortar before final set has taken place.
6. Shuttering removal:  Remove after mortar has cured.

680 Installing intumescent pillows 
1. Number of pillows (per m² of opening):  Number necessary to achieve fire resistance.
2. Orientation of bags:  Parallel to the plane of the construction element containing the opening.

690 Applying intumescent putty 
1. Sequence:  Install putty after services are permanently installed.
2. Loose dust and combustible materials:  Remove from the opening, along with any other debris.
3. Putty cure:  Do not disturb putty before final set has taken place.

04-09-2026 

710 Installing mineral wool batts 
1. Installing batts:  Fit tight into void between the penetrating services and the surrounding 

construction to form a solid barrier. 
1.1. Brackets:  Impale batts on proprietary pressed steel brackets at 500 mm maximum 

centres and not greater than 250 mm from ends of batts.
2. Face of batts:  Flush with the surface of wall, floor or soffit.
3. Joints between batts:  Butt joints; seal with fire-resisting sealant
4. Gaps between services and barrier:  Seal with fire-resisting sealant.

730 Fixing pipe collars 
1. Collar fixing:  Screw fixed, to manufacturers recommendations to suit service conditions.
2. Gap around collar:  In accordance with the manufacturers details and recommendations to 

achieve the performance requirements and to suit the service conditions.
3. Length of wraps:  Project 50 mm from each side of the element.

740 Inserting sealant-backing material 
1. Preparation:  Remove debris from service penetration.
2. Installation:  Insert joint filler to full depth of joint, leaving sufficient depth to apply sealant

745 Applying sealants generally 
1. Application:  As section Z22.

750 Applying capping sealant 
1. Preparation:  Degrease using cleaner recommended by sealant manufacturer
2. Priming:  Primer recommended by sealant manufacturer.
3. Width of sealant:  In accordance with the manufacturers details and recommendations to 

achieve the performance requirements and to suit the service conditions.
4. Depth of sealant:  In accordance with the manufacturers details and recommendations to 

achieve the performance requirements and to suit the service conditions.
5. Temperature:  Do not apply water-based sealants when they could be damaged by frost.

Completion
910 Cleaning

2. Cleaning:  Clean off splashes and droppings. Wipe down finishes.

920 Inspection 
1. Notice for inspection (minimum):  Five working days

190 General Performance 
1. Standards: In accordance with: 

1.1.
• Construction Products Regulations (CPR) 2013 - CE marking.
• Building Regulations Approved Document 7 2013 - CE marking.
• Approved Document Part M of the Building Regulations.
• Code of Practice: Hardware for Fire and Escape Doors' produced jointly by the 

Door and Hardware Federation (DHF) and the GAI.
• BS EN 179 - Emergency exit devices operated by a lever handle or push pad, for 

use on escape routes.
• BS 476: Part 22 - Method for determination of the fire resistance of non-

loadbearing elements of construction. 
• BS EN 1125 - Panic exit devices operated by a horizontal bar, for use on escape 

routes.
• BS EN 1154 - Controlled door closing devices.
• BS EN 1155 - Electrically powered hold-open devices for swing doors.
• BS EN 1158 - Door coordinator devices.
• BS EN 1192 - Doors. Classification of strength requirements.

620 Adverse weather 
1. General   

1.1. Temperature:  Do not lay or joint paving if the temperature is below 3°C on a falling 
thermometer or below 1°C on a rising thermometer.

1.2. Frozen materials:  Do not use. Do not lay bedding on frozen or frost covered bases.
2. Paving with mortar joints and/ or bedding   

2.1. Protect from frost damage, rapid drying out and saturation until mortar has hardened.
Q25

Slab/ Brick/ Sett/ Cobble Pavings
Page 361 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification
Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

3. Paving laid and jointed in sand/ fine aggregate   
3.1. Stockpiled laying course sand/ fine aggregate:  Protect from saturation.
3.2. Exposed areas of unbound laying course and uncompacted areas of unbound 

paving:  Protect from heavy rainfall.
3.3. Saturated unbound laying course:  Remove and replace, or allow to dry before 

proceeding.
3.4. Laying dry sand/ fine aggregate jointed paving in damp conditions:  Brush in as much 

jointing sand as possible. Minimize site traffic over paving. As soon as paving is dry, top up 
joints and complete compaction.

625 Laying pavings – general 
1. Appearance:  Smooth and even with regular joints and accurate to line, level and profile.
2. Falls:  To prevent ponding.
3. Bedding of paving units:  Firm so that rocking or subsidence does not occur or develop. 

3.1. Bedding/ Laying course:  Consistently and accurately graded, spread and compacted to 
produce uniform thickness and support for paving units.

4. Slopes:  Lay paving units upwards from the bottom of slopes.
5. Paving units:  Free of mortar and sand stains.
6. Cutting:  Cut units cleanly and accurately, without spalling, to give neat junctions with edgings 

and adjoining finishes.

630 Levels of paving 
1. Permissible deviation from specified levels   

1.1. Generally:  +/-6 mm.
2. Height of finished paving above features   

2.1. At gullies:  +6 to +10 mm.
2.2. At drainage channels and kerbs:  +3 to +6 mm.

635 Regularity of paved surfaces 
1. Maximum variation in gap under a 3 m straight edge placed anywhere on the surface (where 

appropriate in relation to the geometry of the surface)   
1.1. Precast concrete paving blocks and clay pavers for flexible pavements:  10 mm.
1.2. Precast concrete flags or natural stone slabs:  3 mm.

2. Difference in level between adjacent paving units (maximum):  2 mm.
3. Sudden irregularities:  Not permitted.

637 Regularity of paved surfaces 
1. Maximum undulations in the surface of pavings (except tactile paving surfaces) under a 1 m 

straight edge placed anywhere on the surface (where appropriate in relation to the geometry of 
the surface):  3 mm.

2. Joints between paving units or utility access covers   
2.1. Joints flush with the surface:  Difference in level between adjacent units to be no more 

than twice the joint width (with a 5 mm maximum difference in level).
2.2. Recessed, filled joints:  Difference in level between adjacent units to be no greater than 2 

mm; the recess to be no deeper than 5 mm.

Q25
Slab/ Brick/ Sett/ Cobble Pavings

04-09-2026 

2.3. Unfilled joints:  Difference in level between adjacent units to be no greater than 2 mm.
3. Sudden irregularities:  Not permitted.

640 Colour banding 
1. General: Unless premixed by manufacturer, select from at least 5 separate packs in rotation to 

avoid colour banding.

645 Protection 
1. Cleanliness:  Keep paving clean and free from mortar droppings, oil and other materials likely to 

cause staining.
2. Materials storage:  Do not overload pavings with stacks of materials.
3. Handling:  Do not damage paving unit corners, arrises, or previously laid paving.
4. Mortar-bedded pavings:  Keep free from traffic after laying: 

4.1. Pedestrian traffic (minimum):  Four days
4.2. Vehicular traffic (minimum):  Seven days

5. Access:  Restrict access to paved areas to prevent damage from site traffic and plant.

650 Cementitious bases and sub-bases 
1. General:  Protect from moisture loss, if not covered by another pavement course within 2 hours 

of completion.

655 Condition of sub-bases/ bases before spreading laying course 
1. Trenches and excavation of soft or loose spots in subgrade:  Fill and thoroughly compact.
2. Granular surfaces:  Lay and compact so as to be sound, clean, smooth and close-textured 

enough to prevent migration of bedding/ laying course materials into the sub-base during 
compaction and use, free from movement under compaction plant and free from compaction 
ridges, cracks and loose material.

3. Prepared existing and new bound bases (roadbases):  Sound, clean, free from rutting or major 
cracking. Remove sharp stones, projections and debris.

4. Sub-base/ Roadbase level tolerances:  To BS 7533-101, annex A.
5. Levels and falls:  Accurate and within the specified tolerances.
6. Drainage outlets:  Within 0-10 mm of the required finished level.
7. Features in unbound paving (including mortar-bedded restraints and drainage 

ironwork):  Complete to required levels; adequately bed and haunch in mortar.
8. Sub-bases containing cement/ hydraulic binder:  Cure for minimum times specified in BS 7533-

4.

660 Drainage holes in existing bases 
1. Location:  Impervious layers of existing road/ paving.
2. Drainage:  Form regular grid of holes, through base and any additional build up, down to sub-

base: 
2.1. Spacing in both directions:  750 mm
2.2. Minimum clear opening:  30 mm
2.3. Do not weaken or excessively disturb road/ paving.

725 Laying rigid brick paving 
1. Standard generally:  In accordance with Brick Development Association (BDA) Design Note 8.

3. Cement slurry:  Apply thin slurry (1–3 mm) of neat cement or 1:1 cement:soft sand over the 

freshly laid mortar bed immediately prior to laying bricks.
4. Laying:  Wet bricks as necessary (but do not soak), butter joint faces and press down firmly to 

give a level surface with 10 mm regular joints.

6. Joint profile:  Bucket handle

205 Performance Generally 
1. Achieve a slip resistance value of 36PTV in both dry and wet conditions when carrying out tests 

using a TRL Pendulum Test.
2. Achieve a minima value of 20 μm RZ when carrying out tests with a surface roughness meter.
3. Lift entrances (car and landing doors) to be fire rated.
4. Lift finishes and components to remain stable and in place with no distortion, reduction in 

performance, deformity or change in appearance during operation or use.
5. Upon completion and when in use there shall be no rattling, creaking or shaking of any 

component or finishes system within the lift car.
6. Individual elements of the lift car such as handrail, ceiling panels, control boards and panelling 

are to be removable when in place for maintenance and replacement purposes.

620 Workmanship generally 
1. Lifts to be manufactured at a specialist factory in full compliance with QA/ QC requirements and 

within tolerances shown on the Working Drawings.

shafts are built to the required size within stated tolerances.

finishes.

perfect fit, are square, align and operate efficiently.

the Working Drawings.

Various Materials
To be read with preliminaries/ general conditions.
100 Cement/ Aggregate/ Water/ Sand 
1. Standards: Comply with 

1.1. BS EN 197-6 Cement. Cement with recycled building materials.
1.2. BS EN 1008 Mixing water for concrete - Specification for sampling, testing and assessing 

the suitability of water, including water recovered from processes in the concrete 
industry, as mixing water for concrete.

1.3. BS EN 12620 Aggregates for concrete.
1.4. BS EN 13055 Lightweight aggregates.
1.5. BS EN 13139 Aggregates for mortar.
1.6. PD 6682-3 Aggregates. Aggregates for mortar - guidance on the use of BS EN 13139.
1.7. PD 6682-4 Aggregates. Lightweight aggregates for concrete, mortar and grout - 

Guidance on the use of BS EN 13055-1.
1.8. Glassfibre Reinforced Concrete Association (GRCA) recommendations.

2. Materials 
2.1. Sand:  

2.1.1. Minimise organic impurities.
2.1.2. Restrict the use of deleterious reactive materials containing alkalis in cement.
2.1.3. Use coarse aggregate or dust/ salt/ silt free natural sand.
2.1.4. Less than 10% of material to be able to pass through a 150 micron sieve.
2.1.5. Maintain +/- 20% variation in fineness modulus for facing mix design.
2.1.6. Remove all soluble material.
2.1.7. Carefully control mix variables.

2.2. Water: To remain clean, fresh, potable and impurity free from a known and reliable source.

200 Plasterboard 
1. Standards: Comply with:  

1.1. BS 8000-8 Workmanship on construction sites. Design and installation of dry lining 
systems - Code of practice.

1.2. BS EN 520 Gypsum plasterboards - Definitions, requirements and test methods.
1.3. GPDA (Gypsum Products Development Association) recommendations.
1.4. FIS (Finishes and Interiors Sector) recommendations.

2. Materials: 
2.1. Quality:  

2.1.1. To be high performance type of 1500kg/m2 density when used in high humidity/ 
damp areas and/ or when walls are to receive tiling, linings and the like.

2.1.2. In areas likely to be wet or very damp use moisture resistant plasterboard.
2.1.3. Use Type A/ H/ E and P where standard sheets are required.

Z01
Various Materials
Page 381 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification
Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

2.1.4. Use Type I in high density areas such as cores where glass fibre is required as 
reinforcement.

2.1.5. Metal foil backed sheets are not acceptable as a water/ vapour barrier.
2.1.6. Before applying wet render of plaster to moisture resistant boards without suitable 

pre-treatment with an appropriate bonding agent. Such treatment to be agreed in 
advance.

2.1.7. All boards to be new.
3. Installation: 

3.1. Workmanship: 
3.1.1. Multiple layer boarding to be staggered by 600mm.
3.1.2. Boards to be undamaged, clean and dry with no sign of having been wet prior to 

installation.
3.1.3. Fix to suitably prepared and stable framework/ substrate.
3.1.4. Only install in dry interior areas.
3.1.5. Joints to be tight butts without any lipping, imperfect edges or indentations.
3.1.6. Cuts to be straight and neat.
3.1.7. Cut edges to be lightly sanded, sealed and smooth forming a tight continuous fit.
3.1.8. Avoid using cut boards at perimeter conditions or at edges of penetrations.
3.1.9. Use only recommended and appropriate fixings installed strictly in accordance with 

the manufacturer’s recommendations.
3.1.10. Apply fixings at appropriate centres commencing in the middle of each board.
3.1.11. Final surfaces to be smooth and flat without indentations caused by fixings.
3.1.12. Taper edge joints and butt each sheet with a minimal gap of no more than 2mm.
3.1.13. Stagger joints either side of the partition.
3.1.14. Install appropriate flush beading/ reinforcement at edges, openings, perimeters, 

interfaces, stress points, etc. where necessary.
3.1.15. Tape and fill all joints/ gaps to provide a smooth, flat and continuous surface finish 

using 100% secured tape and apply plaster skim coat of appropriate thickness.

1.43. Approved Document Part B - Building Regulations - Bitumen impregnated fibreboard.
1.44. BFP (British Plastic Federation) - recommendations for laminated sheets.
1.45. BLFA (British Laminate Fabricators Association) - recommendations for laminated sheets.

2. Materials: 
2.1. General: Board grade to be sufficiently and suitably robust, sound, of suitable class/ 

thickness and moisture resistant to suit prevailing conditions and use.
2.2. Fibreboard: Only use bitumen impregnated fibreboard with cavity barriers.  
2.3. Laminated faced panels:  

2.3.1. Core substrate to be of suitable type and quality of either:
• Plywood.
• Grade P5 particleboard.
• MDF.
• Blockboard.
• Laminboard.

2.3.2. Control moisture content to suit prevailing environmental conditions and core 
material.

2.3.3. Bond sheets in presses.
2.3.4. Fully condition sheets prior to lamination.
2.3.5. Oversize holes to compensate for shrinkage of the laminate.
2.3.6. Final board thickness (including laminate) to provide:

• Sufficient structural strength.
• Impact resistance.
• Stability.

2.3.7. Laminated panels to be balanced to stop warping, bowing, etc.
2.3.8. Allow a minimum of 36 hours atmospheric conditioning prior to commencement of 

high pressure laminate bonding.
2.3.9. All edges to be smooth cut and match adjoining panels/ surfaces.
2.3.10. Apply laminate to edges first followed by main surfaces.
2.3.11. Edge and top joints to be chamfered at 45°.
2.3.12. Black lines at edges are not acceptable.
2.3.13. Final finish to be from from bowing/ twisting/ chips/ pimpling/ scratches/ cracks/ 

surface contamination, debris and marks/ depressions/ stains/ colour, texture and 
pattern irregularities.

2.3.14. Edge conditions to be agreed as either:
• Hardwood lipping.
• Post formed edges.
• 3mm thick PVC in matching or agreed colour.
• Decorative laminate in matching or agreed colour, using radio frequency 

edgebanders.
• No edging.

3. Workmanship: 
3.1. Board fixing:  

3.1.1. Run edges along supports.
3.1.2. At 150mm c/c maximum to intermediate supports.

Page 384 of 444

Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

3.1.3. At 100mm c/c maximum to edge boards.
3.1.4. Inset 12mm from edge.

3.2. Plywood:  
3.2.1. Take care storing on site to ensure protection from adverse environmental 

conditions with free airflow maintained at all times.
3.2.2. Fix plywood:

• Long edges running at 90° to supports.
• End joints to be staggered in alignment with supports.
• 150mm c/c at perimeters/ edges.
• 300mm c/c at intermediate supports.
• Use 50mm x 3mm annular ring shank nails.
• Bond floor joints using PVA adhesive.

3.3. Chipboard:  
3.3.1. Long edges running at 90° to supports.
3.3.2. End joints to be staggered in alignment with supports.
3.3.3. 300mm c/c at perimeters/ edges/ interim supports.
3.3.4. Use 50mm x 3mm annular ring shank nails.
3.3.5. Bond floor joints using PVA adhesive.
3.3.6. Leave a 10mm gap around all perimeter edges for expansion.

3.4. Laminated panels: No lipping with flush joints and flat surfaces panel to panel in all 
directions.

2. Materials 
2.1. General:  

2.1.1. Use only float process to manufacture annealed glass.
2.1.2. Glass to be clear, undistorted and free from reflection.
2.1.3. Glass packaging to show place of manufacture.
2.1.4. Glass to be sourced from a single source for each element of the Works.
2.1.5. Only use kitemarked safety glass.
2.1.6. Glass to be free from impurities.
2.1.7. Glass/ glazing systems to be fully warranted and guaranteed to suit design and 

service life requirements.
2.1.8. Glass/ glazing systems to be suitable for intended use, of an appropriate type/ 

quality, of adequate thickness and to an acceptable finish.
2.1.9. Glass/ glazing systems to comply with all performance and visual intent 

requirements.
2.1.10. Mirror glass is not acceptable unless specifically requested.
2.1.11. Glazing installations to be visually consistent and acceptable throughout, 

maintaining the appearance and quality of all accepted samples, prototypes and 
benchmarks.

2.1.12. Visual acceptance to be determined by observation/ inspection from a distance of 
1000mm at multiple angles with both blue sky and cloudy backgrounds.

2.1.13. The following are unacceptable:
• Visual distortion.
• Air bubbles.
• Imperfections of any kind.
• Smoke vanes.

2.1.14. Glazing systems to be manufactured in specialist facilities using appropriate 
equipment to ensure pristine accuracy in terms of cutting, finishing, assembly, etc.

2.1.15. Edges, joints, to be smooth, even, ground and arrised to provide a visually 
acceptable product.

Z01
Various Materials
Page 388 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification
Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

2.1.16. Assemblies to be installed safely into frames within the recommended/ agreed 
tolerances as shown on the Working Drawings.

2.1.17. Panels to be designed to facilitate individual removal for maintenance/ removal 
purposes.

2.1.18. Glazing systems are not to exert stress or external force on any component/ 
assembly in any condition that reduces its performance, visual appearance, shape 
or safety.

2.1.19. Coatings and finishes to remain pristine.
2.1.20. Carry out full stress analysis to confirm compliance.
2.1.21. Wired glass to be minimum 3mm thick, using clear annealed sheet glass with 1.3mm 

gauge georgian wire embedded during glass manufacture.
2.1.22. Fire resistant safety glass to be Category B/ Class 2B2 minimum being clear and 

unwired incorporating fire rated beading.
2.2. Laminated glass:  

2.2.1. To be carried out at a specialist facility.
2.2.2. Bond a minimum of two layers of flat glass with a suitable clear interlayer between 

each panel.
2.2.3. Accurately align each sheet to prevent overlapping at edges.
2.2.4. No distortions are acceptable.
2.2.5. Support bottom edges such that loads are supported evenly along the full length of 

each unit.
2.2.6. Point loading is not acceptable.
2.2.7. Fully seal all edges.

2.3. Heat strengthening glass: 
2.3.1. To be carried out at a specialist facility.
2.3.2. Fully control strengthening process and anisotropy to avoid reduction in 

performance, quality or visual intent.
2.3.3. Edge dip not to exceed 0.25mm/300mm.
2.3.4. Glass edge bowing to be less than 0.12mm/300mm.
2.3.5. Flatness criteria to be 1mm in 1000mm in any direction and non accumulative over 

3000mm.
2.3.6. Minimise roller wave distortion such that it is not visible/ detectable in any direction/ 

orientation.
2.3.7. Small visible ripples may be accepted provided that they are horizontally consistent 

and evenly spaced over the whole panel.
2.4. Toughened glass: 

2.4.1. Generally unacceptable unless agreed in advance in writing.
2.4.2. Must be heat soaked, tested with full audit traceability and audit trail.
2.4.3. Provide full technical details and guarantee of performance, suitability and 

adherence to visual intent if toughening is proposed.
2.5. Curved glass: 

2.5.1. To be carried out at a specialist facility.
2.5.2. Maintain a consistent radius.
2.5.3. Use specially prepared and appropriate types of glass. Demonstrate compliance 

through calculations and samples/ prototypes.
Z01

Various Materials
Page 389 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification
Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

2.5.4. Maintain performance requirements, visual intent and quality.
2.5.5. Maintain tight tolerances to guarantee a consistent curve when multiple panels and 

connected. 
2.5.6. Maintain perfect alignment at vertical and horizontal edges which are to be 

continuous with consistent joint widths.
2.5.7. Maximum allowable deviation in curvature of a complete installation to be a 

maximum of ±4mm from the theoretical position.
2.6. Mirror glass: To be: 

2.6.1. New.
2.6.2. Clean.
2.6.3. Flat.
2.6.4. Clear.
2.6.5. Smooth.
2.6.6. Free of defects, marks or blemishes.
2.6.7. Completely undistorted.

2.7. Horizontal/ inclined glazing: 
2.7.1. Use only suitably strengthened glass.
2.7.2. Fix into a suitable and adequate support framing system which is structurally sound.
2.7.3. Fixing/ restraining methods to guarantee a safe installation.
2.7.4. Performance requirements and visual intent to be maintained.
2.7.5. Glass to remain in place (i.e. no pieces of glass or support system will fall to the 

ground) should damage or breakage occur.
2.7.6. System to be sufficiently sound to resist all live and dead loads to which it is 

subjected including accidental damage, maintenance, cleaning, etc. 
2.7.7. Carry out impact tests to an energy level of 1200J minimum.

2.1.12. Used as part of a complete waterproofing system.

2.5.4. Membrane to be fleece backed.
2.5.5. Suitable for use in conditions of exposure to Ultra Violet light and direct sunlight.

3. Workmanship: 
3.1. Installed membranes to provide a watertight barrier at all conditions and interfaces.
3.2. Lay on adequately and suitably prepared base which:

• Sufficiently flat, smooth and without voids or sharp protrusions.
• When laid on hardcore shall be blinded with 12mm sift sand or other suitable 

material and a 50mm standard mix concrete screed (GEN 1 or ST1/ST2).
3.3. Lay membrane flat, smooth and fully bonded/ mechanically using suitable fixings and flat 

smooth galvanised steel bars/ washers to substrate without air bubbles, ridges, ripples, 
etc.

3.4. Membrane to be suitably lapped and sealed at all joints. 
3.5. Laps to be staggered with a minimum 75mm overlap at edges and a minimum 150mm at 

ends.
3.6. Watertight seals to be provided at all interfaces including drains, services and structural 

penetrations.
3.7. Use adequate protection boards where necessary.
3.8. Tears or punctures to be repaired immediately or replaced to guarantee future 

performance and watertightness. Report all such instances.
3.9. Use primers as appropriate and necessary in accordance with the manufacturer’s 

recommendations.
3.10. Installation to accommodate without reduction in performance all movement to which it is 

exposed including those resulting from thermal expansion, moisture, structure, 
environmental conditions, etc.

3. Workmanship 
3.1. Install to line and level to provide a flat and horizontal/ vertical surface.
3.2. Alignment of slabs to be precise with no lipping, inconsistent joints or change of level.
3.3. Cuts to be straight and accurate.
3.4. Edges to be straight and smooth with no imperfections.
3.5. No surface marking or visual imperfections.
3.6. Apply cured sealer coats as required to dry, clean and dust free surfaces.
3.7. Sealer to be completely dry before application of further coats.
3.8. Control areas to be sealed so as to maintain consistency of finish and appearance.
3.9. Replace areas not accepted in terms of workmanship quality, finish or visual intent.

Fabrication
515 Fabrication generally 
1. Contact between dissimilar metals in components:  Avoid.
2. Finished components:  Rigid and free from distortion, cracks, burrs and sharp arrises. 

2.1. Moving parts:  Free moving without binding.
3. Corner junctions of identical sections:  Mitre.
4. Aluminium/ metal sheets:  To be ridged fully welded/ sealed, tempered, and visually flat (1:1000 

over 3000mm in all directions), without distortion.
5. Corners:  Corners to comprise purpose made components and not cut unless shown otherwise 

on the Working Drawings. Colour and finish to match adjoining elements.
6. Protection:  Provide adequate protection at all times.
7. Insulation:  Provide, where necessary, to negate drumming and other noise.
8. Joints:  To be concealed, continuous and sealed.
9. Ends:  Aluminium ends to be mill finished. Metal ends to be hot dipped galvanised.

520 Cold formed work 
1. Profiles:  Accurate, with straight arrises.

525 Adhesive bonding 
1. Preparation of surfaces of metals to receive adhesives   

1.1. Degrease.
1.2. Abrade mechanically or chemically etch.
1.3. Prime:  To suit adhesive.

2. Adhesive bond:  Form under pressure.

527 Welding 
1. Description:  Continuous and suitable.
2. Welding procedures   

2.1. Method and standard:  Full penetration welds, flush ground where visible. Use jigs/ clamps, 
as necessary, to ensure a precise fit.

2.2. Welding Procedure Specification (WPS):  Provide written procedure statement.
3. Preparation   

3.1. Joint preparation:  Clean thoroughly.
3.2. Surfaces of materials that will be self-finished and visible in the completed work:  protect 

from weld splatter, residue build up and splatter.
4. Jointing   

4.1. Joints:  Fully bond parent and filler metal throughout with no inclusions, holes, porosity or 
cracks.

4.2. Dissimilar metals:  Use appropriate material and techniques.

Z11
Purpose Made Metalwork

Page 408 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification
Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

4.3. Strength requirements:  Welds to achieve design loads.
4.4. Heat straightening:  Only when agreed in advance.
4.5. Complex assemblies:  Agree priority for welding members to minimize distortion caused 

by subsequent welds.
4.6. Tack welds:  Use only for temporary attachment.
4.7. Jigs:  Provide to support and restrain members during welding.
4.8. Filler plates:  Obtain approval.
4.9. Lap joints:  Minimum 5 x metal thickness or 25 mm, whichever is greater.
4.10. Weld terminations:  Clean and sound.
4.11. Workmanship:  Visible holes, inclusions, ridges, cracks, undercuts or porosity are not 

acceptable.
4.12. Stainless steel welds: 

4.12.1. Grind to leave smooth and seamless surface.
4.12.2. Remove heat tints using light abrasives, wire brushing, pickling paste or other 

similar and acceptable method.
4.12.3. Comply with BS 499 series of standards and BS EN 1011 series of standards.
4.12.4. Minimise distortion during welding.
4.12.5. All welds, methods and consumables to achieve, as a minimum, the same level of 

corrosion resistance as the base material.

530 Stainless steel fabrication 
1. Guillotining or punching:  Do not use for metal thicknesses greater that 10 mm.
2. Thermal cutting   

2.1. Carbonation in the heat affected zone:  Remove, after cutting.
3. Bending   

3.1. Plates or bars:  Cold bending radius not less than material thickness.
3.2. Tubes:  Cold bending radius not less than 2 x tube diameter.

4. Welding:  In addition to general welding requirements: 
4.1. Protect adjacent surfaces from weld spatter.
4.2. Pickle all welds before post fabrication treatments.

5. Protection:  Provide protection to fabricated components during transit and on site.
6. Corrosion:  Not acceptable.

555 Brazing 
1. Standard:  To BS EN 14324 Brazing. Guidance on the application of brazed joints.
2. Testing   

2.1. Destructive testing:  To BS EN 12797 Brazing - Destructive tests of brazed joints.
2.2. Nondestructive testing:  To BS EN 12799 Brazing. Non-destructive examination of brazed 

joints.

310 Fasteners generally 
1. Materials need to have: 

1.1. Bimetallic corrosion resistance appropriate to items being fixed.
1.2. Atmospheric corrosion resistance appropriate to fixing location.
1.3. Fire resistance appropriate to fixing location and application.
1.4. Material safety and data sheets (MSDS).
1.5. Suitable strength and properties to safely accommodate attachments and movements.
1.6. To be appropriate, secure and firmly fixed in place.
1.7. Bowing, distortion, vibration and other movement is not acceptable.
1.8. Unless shown otherwise on the Working Drawings, fixings are not to be visible.
1.9. Fixings to aluminium frames and cappings to match material to which they are attached.
1.10. Removable items to have invisible secret fixings unless shown otherwise on the Working 

Drawings.
1.11. Fixings to comply with all relevant Standards and Codes of Practice, being suitable for 

their intended purpose in terms of strength and type.
1.12. Maximise use of Allen headed bolts on proprietary systems.
1.13. Fixing systems to be complete and include all accessories necessary such as bearings, 

brackets, flashings, closures etc.
1.14. Galvanise steel subframe assemblies.
1.15. When fixing to concrete utilise cast-in channels as appropriate.
1.16. When fixing to structural steel use direct fixings as appropriate.

Execution
610 Fixing generally 
1. Integrity of supported components:  Select types, sizes, quantities and spacings of fixings, 

fasteners and packings to retain supported components without distortion or loss of support.
2. Components, substrates, fixings and fasteners of dissimilar metals:  Isolate with washers/ 

sleeves to avoid bimetallic corrosion.
3. Fixing to structure:  Record all primary fixings to include size, type, spacing, embedment, 

precise location, edge dimensions, shimming, isolation techniques for dissimilar metals, locking 
washers, etc. and test results.

4. Bolts:  After tightening, leave a minimum of one clear thread above each nut.
5. Appearance:  Fixings to be in straight lines at regular centres.
6. Compliance:  To Approved Document Part A of the Building Regulations.
7. Tools:  Use purpose made tooling systems where appropriate and necessary to ensure 

consistent and adequate depths and perpendicular installation.
8. Screws:  Countersunk screw heads visible in the finished works require countersinks to be 

machined in order to receive them.

625 Fixing using clips 
1. Fastener spacing:  In accordance with system manufacturers details/ recommendations and 

the requirements of the testing/ certification criteria applicable to each system to achieve the 
specific performance requirements and to suit the service conditions of each individual 
instance.

2. Accessories :  As necessary to complete the system design and installation, achieve the 
performance requirements and to suit the service conditions

630 Fixing packings 
1. Function:  To take up tolerances and prevent distortion of materials and components.
2. Limits:  Do not use packings beyond thicknesses recommended by fixings and fasteners 

manufacturer.
3. Locations:  Not within zones to be filled with sealant.
4. Quality: 

4.1. Use only rot-proof, non-corrodible and non-compressible materials next to fixing points. 
4.2. Packings not to be visible.
4.3. In floors, allow 10mm gap at each edge, parallel to the lie of the boards.

640 Fixing cramps 
1. Cramp positions:  Maximum 150 mm from each end of frame sections and at 600 mm maximum 

centres.
2. Fasteners:  Fix cramps to frames with screws of same material as cramps.
3. Fixings in masonry work:  Fully bed in mortar.

Fixings and Adhesives

Page 419 of 444



1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification
Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

650 Fixing with nails 
1. Fastener spacing:  In accordance with the manufacturers details and recommendations to 

achieve the performance requirements and to suit the service conditions. 
1.1. Marking :  Mark locations of the nails on the substrate surface.

2. Pilot holes:  Drill using a hammer drill. 
2.1. Diameter:  0.2 mm larger than diameter of nail.
2.2. Depth:  20mm.
2.3. Cleaning:  Blow out holes with compressed air of a minimum 80 psi for four seconds to 

remove all debris and dust or brush out holes with a nylon brush for a minimum of four 
cycles to remove all debris and dust.

3. Installation:  Drive nail in without splitting or crushing timber.
4. Finished level of nail head 

4.1. Exposed:  Flush with surface
4.2. Punched (holes filled or stopped):  Sink minimum 2 mm below surface.

660 Screw fixing 
1. Fastener spacing :  In accordance with the manufacturers details and recommendations to 

achieve the performance requirements and to suit the service conditions. 
1.1. Marking:  Mark locations of the screws on the substrate surface.

2. Pilot holes :  Drill using a hammer drill. 
2.1. Diameter:  0.2 mm larger than diameter of the screw.
2.2. Depth:  20mm.
2.3. Cleaning:  Blow out holes with compressed air of a minimum 80 psi for four seconds to 

remove all debris and dust or brush out holes with a nylon brush for a minimum of four 
cycles to remove all debris and dust.

3. Installation:  Dive screw into pilot hole using a drill. Ensure not to split or damage the timber.
4. Finished level of countersunk screw heads   

4.1. Exposed:  Flush with surface.
4.2. Concealed (holes filled or stopped):  Sink minimum 2 mm below surface.
4.3. Hardwood:  Drill pilot holes to half the diameter of the shank when using 8 gauge (or more) 

screws.
5. Testing:   

670 Pelleted countersunk screw fixing 
1. Finished level of countersunk screw heads:  Minimum 6 mm below timber surface.
2. Pellets:  Cut from matching timber, match grain and glue in to full depth of hole.
3. Finished level of pellets:  Flush with surface.

680 Plugged countersunk screw fixing 
1. Finished level of countersunk screw heads:  Minimum 6 mm below timber surface.
2. Plugs:  Glue in to full depth of hole.
3. Finished level of plugs:  Projecting above surface.

690 Using powder actuated fixing systems 
1. Powder actuated fixing tools:  To BS 4078-2 Powder actuated fixing systems. Specification for 

tools and Kitemark certified.
2. Operatives:  Trained and certified as competent by tool manufacturer.
3. Primer:  Apply zinc rich primer to external fastener heads or locations subject to possible 

dampness.
4. Isolation:  Isolate stainless steel components from external cartridge fired nails using top hat 

section plastic washers.

700 Applying adhesives 
1. Surface preparation:  Dry, clean, and free from oil, grease and other contaminants. Adjust 

regularity and texture to suit bonding and gap filling characteristics of adhesive. 
2. Temporary support and clamping:  Provide as necessary. Do not mark surfaces of (or distort) 

components being fixed.
3. Finished joints:  Fully bonded and free of surplus adhesive.
4. Conditions:  Do not use during unsuitable climatic/ environmental conditions, after 

recommended “use by” date/ open pot times.
5. Other requirements:  In accordance with the manufacturers details and recommendations to 

achieve the performance requirements and to suit the service conditions.
6. Application 

6.1. Use the appropriate primer which is left to dry before adhesive is applied.
6.2. Use recommended spreaders/ applicators/ tools to ensure adequate and even coverage.
6.3. Remove surplus adhesive before it dries.

Z21
Mortars
Cement gauged mortars
110 Cement gauged mortar mixes 
1. Specification:  Proportions and additional requirements for mortar materials are specified 

elsewhere.
2. Standards: 

2.1. To BS EN 1996-1-1 Eurocode 6 - Design of masonry structures. General rules for 
reinforced and unreinforced masonry structures. Reference Table NA2.

2.2. To BS EN 998-1 Specification for mortar for masonry. Rendering and plastering mortar.
2.3. To BS EN 998-2 Specification for mortar for masonry. Masonry mortar.

3. Appearance:  No colour difference above and below DPC level or between different daily 
installations.

4. Performance:  Mix to meet performance requirements in all conditions and locations.
5. Workmanship: 

5.1. Mix thoroughly in a suitable forced action mechanical mixer to produce uniform/ 
consistent colour and workable consistency.

5.2. Do not use a free fall drum mixer.
5.3. Minimise water content whilst maintaining required consistency/ workability.
5.4. Batch by volume using dry sand.
5.5. Maintain a mix ratio of 1:3.5/ 4 cement to sand when mixing semi-dry mortar and ensure 

that it does not crumble or deform when squeezed or form a film of water on the surface.
5.6. Maintain a mix ratio of no less than 1:3.5/ 4 cement to sand when mixing a floating mortar 

bed and ensure that it does not crumble or deform when squeezed or form a film of water 
on the surface. Lay on a smooth and thoroughly cleaned base material with a minimum 
500 gauge polythene separating lay over insulation with minimum 100mm laps at joints. 
Mix to be semi-dry incorporating steel mesh as necessary.

6. Proprietary mortars:  Ensure that allowance is made for selection/addition of project-specific 
aggregate such as may be required to obtain the visual acceptance of the Architect.

120 Sand for site made cement gauged masonry mortars 
1. Standard:  To BS EN 13139:2002 Aggregates for mortar.
2. Grading:  0/2 (FP or MP). 

2.1. Fines content where the proportion of sand in a mortar mix is specified as a range (e.g. 
1:1:5-6): 

2.1.1. Lower proportion of sand:  Use category 3 fines.
2.1.2. Higher proportion of sand:  Use category 2 fines.

3. Sand for facework mortar:  Maintain consistent colour and texture. Obtain from one source.
4. Marine sand:  Do not use with sulphate resisting or super sulphated cement.

131 Ready-Mixed lime:sand for cement gauged masonry mortars 
1. Standard:  To BS EN 998-2 Specification for mortar for masonry. Masonry mortar.

190 Retarded ready to use cement gauged mortar

200 Storage of cement gauged mortar materials 
1. Sands and aggregates:  Keep different types/ grades in separate stockpiles on hard, clean, 

free-draining bases.
2. Factory made ready-mixed lime:sand/ ready to use retarded mortars:  Keep in covered 

containers to prevent drying out or wetting.
3. Bagged cement/ hydrated lime:  Store off the ground in dry conditions.

210 Making cement gauged mortars 
1. Batching:  By volume. Use clean and accurate gauge boxes or buckets. 

1.1. Mix proportions:  Based on dry sand. Allow for bulking of damp sand.
2. Mixing:  Mix materials thoroughly to uniform consistency, free from lumps. 

2.1. Mortars containing air entraining admixtures:  Mix mechanically. Do not overmix.
3. Working time (maximum):  Two hours at normal temperatures.
4. Contamination:  Prevent intermixing with other materials.

250 Grout 
1. Batching:  By volume. Use clean and accurate gauge boxes or buckets.
2. General: 

2.1. To be latex modified, pigmented and colour matched.
2.2. To be impervious, resistant to chemical attack/ mould, non-staining and of a type that is a 

proprietary product manufactured by a specialist manufacturer.
3. Sealer:  To be an impervious, uncontaminated, clear water based proprietary product which 

does not have any detrimental effect on the grout (performance or colour).
4. Staining:  Not acceptable.

500 Testing

4.4. Replace and re test any walling erected using mortar which fails a test.

510 Workmanship 
1. Storage:  Store materials in a dry location, raised above ground and under cover to protect from 

rain, damp, frost, etc.
2. Equipment:  Keep all tools and equipment clean and in good working order.
3. Techniques:  Fully trained operatives to employ tried and tested techniques to achieve a 

workable, lump free and appropriate/ consistent mix. Mix by suitable mechanical means using 
dry sand and mortar containing air-entraining admixtures where appropriate or recommended 
by the manufacturer.

4. Weather conditions:  Mortar temperature to be a minimum of 4℃ at all times. Replace mortar 
which has been affected by frost or temperatures below 4℃.

Execution
610 Suitability of joints 
1. Presealing checks   

1.1. Joint dimensions:  Within limits specified for the sealant.
1.2. Substrate quality:  Surfaces regular, undamaged and stable.

2. Primer:  Joints to be carefully primed, without spillage or seeping, to achieve a full bond, without 
causing damage or change in appearance. Use masking tape where necessary.

620 Preparing joints 
1. Surfaces to which sealant must adhere   

1.1. Remove temporary coatings, tapes, loosely adhering material, dust, oil, grease, surface 
water and contaminants that may affect bond.

1.2. Clean using materials and methods recommended by sealant manufacturer.
1.3. Non porous surfaces such as metal, to be cleaned with an appropriate chemical agent, 

which does not damage the substrate or sealant in any way.
2. Vulnerable surfaces adjacent to joints:  Mask to prevent staining or smearing with primer or 

sealant.
3. Backing strip and/ or bond breaker installation:  Insert into joint to correct depth, without 

stretching or twisting, leaving no gaps.
4. Protection:  Keep joints clean and protect from damage until sealant is applied.

630 Applying sealants 
1. Substrate:  Dry (unless recommended otherwise) and unaffected by frost, ice or snow.

2. Trade recommendations:  In accordance with the “Industry Guide to the Professional 
Application of Construction Sealants on Site” published by BASA (British Adhesives and

3. Environmental conditions:  Do not dry or raise temperature of joints by heating.
4. Sealant application:  Fill joints evenly, completely and neatly, ensuring firm adhesion to 

substrates, without bubbling using appropriate tools.
5. Sealant profiles   

5.1. Butt and lap joints:  Slightly concave.
5.2. Fillet joints:  Flat or slightly convex.

6. Joint fillers: 
6.1. To be entirely compatible and suitable.
6.2. Gaps in joints to be consistent with depth of sealant.
6.3. Sealant cross section in joint to be a ratio of 2:1 - width to depth - unless agreed 

otherwise.
6.4. Generate fillers from closed cell foam.
6.5. Fillers are not to adhere to cured sealant - use bond breaker tape if necessary.

7. Protection:  Protect finished joints from contamination or damage until sealant has cured.

against corrosion of iron and steel in structures. General principles of design and 
corrosion resistance.

1.29. BS EN ISO 14713-3 Zinc coatings - guidelines and recommendations for the protection 
against corrosion of iron and steel in structures. Sherardizing.

1.30. BS EN ISO 17834 Thermal spraying Coatings for protection against corrosion and 
oxidation at elevated temperatures.

1.31. BS EN ISO 28706-1 Vitreous and porcelain enamels - Determination of resistance to

room temperature.

1.32. BS EN ISO 28706-2 Vitreous and porcelain enamels — Determination of resistance to

applied to steel panels intended for architecture.

1.34. BS EN ISO 28764 Vitreous and porcelain enamels - Production of specimens for testing

302 Preparation/ quality 
1. Surfaces to receive treatment to be:  

1.1. Bare metal.
1.2. Clean and free from any existing surface finish.
1.3. Smooth and regular.
1.4. Uncontaminated.
1.5. Consistent in terms of colour and texture.
1.6. Uniform.
1.7. Unblemished and undistorted.
1.8. Free of scratches, dents or other imperfections.

2. Finished surfaces to be:  
2.1. Of acceptable quality and finish.
2.2. Colour and texture consistent/ uniform.
2.3. Resistant to fading, prevailing environmental conditions.
2.4. Stable.
2.5. Unaffected by UV light.
2.6. Free of surface imperfection.

3. Allow for metallics, including RAL 9006 and RAL 9007. 30% gloss. Allow for bespoke colour 
matching to samples held at the Architect's office. 

305 Galvanised Finishes 
1. Workmanship/ Quality:  

1.1. Evenly applied to an agreed, uniform and appropriate thickness to achieve the design 
intent and visual requirements.

1.2. Smooth, uniform and consistent with no edges, ridges, burrs and the like.
1.3. Clean with no staining or visible drip marks.
1.4. Fill pinholes prior to application.
1.5. Confirm suitability of steel to be coated prior to galvanising, including suitability for acid 

pickling in diluted hydrochloric acid and passivated if required.
1.6. Single dip application only.
1.7. Do not touch up and repair any coated surface without prior written agreement.
1.8. Do not use Galvafroid or any paint applied finish.
1.9. Minimise distortion and keep within visual tolerances that are acceptable.

306 Vitreous enamel finishes 
1. Base material: 

1.1. To be decarbonised.
1.2. Maximum 0.006% carbon content allowable unless stabilised by addition of appropriate 

amount of titanium. All to be agreed in advance.
1.3. Internal stresses resulting from cold rolling of steel are not acceptable.
1.4. Corrosion free.
1.5. Clause 302 above applies as relevant.

General Metalwork Finishes

1198 - St James's Market Phase 2 – Architectural Specification
Client: The Crown Estate
04-09-2026 

2. Finish: 
2.1. Colour, texture and finish to be consistent and acceptable.
2.2. Variance of gloss levels to remain within seven units of that agreed.
2.3. Mirroring is not acceptable.

3. Quality/ workmanship 
3.1. All surfaces to be fully coated.
3.2. Welding material to be consistent and ground smooth at all locations including corners 

and whirls.
3.3. Maintain full coating/ testing records on site prior to installation for checking when 

requested.

4.3. Scope of testing:  
• Impact resistance.
• Permeability/ water ingress.

• Chalking, colour retention and finish.
• Mortar resistance.

• Panel size for testing to be a minimum of 200mm x 100mm.
• Extrusion size for testing to be a minimum of 300mm.

210 Working procedures 
1. Comply with the following standards

1.2. Steel components:  BS EN 13438.

1.4. Health and safety guidance:  Health and Safety Executive publication Reducing risk 

associated with using coating powders - employers.
1.5. Adequacy:  Ensure aluminium temper and thickness are suitable to receive treatment 

without reducing performance or colour consistency of the application.
1.6. Use one, spray applied batch per colour.
1.7. Use regulated and automated equipment at all times.
1.8. Dry film thickness between panels not to exceed 20%.
1.9. Maintain colour consistency across all panels and extrusions.

310 Pretreatment of aluminium components 
1. Condition of components to be pretreated   

1.1. Free from corrosion and damage
1.2. All welding and jointing completed and finish off as specified
1.3. Free from impurities including soil, grease and oil

2.1. Chromate system:  BS EN 12206-1.

1. Condition of components to be pretreated   

1.1. Free from corrosion and damage
1.2. All welding and jointing completed and finish off as specified
1.3. Free from impurities including soil, grease and oil
1.4. Suitable for and compatible with the pretreatment process

3. Rinsing requirements:  Use demineralized water. Drain and dry.

330 Pretreatment for protection in aggressive environments 
1. Minimum thickness of 60 micrometres across significant and/ or primary surfaces
2. All cut edges, drilled holes and mitres to be fully sealed
3. Cleaning and maintenance:  Carried out once every three to 12 months (dependent on 

proximity to pollutant).

430 Extent of powder coatings 
1. Application:  To visible component surfaces, and concealed surfaces requiring protection. 

Coated surfaces will be deemed 'significant surfaces' for relevant BS EN 13438 performance 
requirements.

435 Application of powder coatings 
1. Surfaces to receive powder coatings:  Free from dust or powder deposits.
2. Powder colours:  Obtain from one batch of one manufacturer.
3. Commencement of powder-coating:  To be continuous from pretreatment.
4. Components to be installed on site in order of application.
5. Jig points:  Not visible on coated components.
6. Curing:  Controlled to attain metal temperatures and hold periods recommended by powder-

coating manufacturer.
7. Stripping and recoating of components:  Only acceptable by prior agreement of powder-

coating manufacturer. Stripping, pretreatment and powder-coating are to be in accordance 
with manufacturer's requirements.

8. Overcoating of components:  Not acceptable.

510 Protection

installation, or by subsequent site operations.
2. Protective coverings must be 

2.1. Resistant to weather conditions
2.2. Partially removable to suit building in and access to fixing points

3.1. Low tack, self adhesive and light in colour
3.2. Applied and removed in accordance with tape and powder-coating manufacturers' 

recommendations. Do not use solvents to remove residues as these are detrimental to 
the coating

4. Inspection of protection:  Carry out monthly. Promptly repair any deterioration or deficiency.

105 Standards 
1. Comply with: 

1.1. BS 3987 Specification for anodic oxidation coatings on wrought aluminium for external 
architectural applications.

1.2. BS 6001-1 Sampling procedures for inspection by attributes. Sampling schemes indexed 
by acceptance quality limit (AQL) for lot-by-lot inspection - Level 2 general inspection.

1.3. BS ISO 2859-2 Sampling procedures for inspection by attributes. Sampling plans indexed 
by limiting quality (LQ) for isolated lot inspection.

1.4. BS EN ISO 2360 - Coating thickness for non conductive coatings on non magnetic 
electrically conductive base metal using amplitude sensitive Eddy Current method.

1.5. BS EN ISO 2931 Anodizing of aluminium and its alloys. Assessment of quality of sealed 
anodic oxidation coatings by measurement of admittance.

1.6. BS EN ISO 7599 Anodizing of aluminium and its alloys - Method for specifying decorative 
and protective anodic oxidation coatings on aluminium.

1.7. BS EN ISO 8251 Anodizing of aluminium and its alloys. Measurement of abrasion 
resistance of anodic oxidation coatings - Testing - Wear index <1.4.

110 Anodic coating 
1. Selected anodizer:  Submit details before commencement of anodizing, including: 

1.1. Name and contact details.
1.2. Details of accreditation schemes.
1.3. Technical data of product, including current Agrément certificates.

2. Process:    
2.1. Two stage electrolytic using cobalt or nickel colouring agent. (Note - single stage integral 

process may be used with prior authorisation).
2.2. Do not use tin electrolytes.
2.3. Consistency of finish and colour to be the same throughout.
2.4. Alloy extrusions to be Grade AW-6063.
2.5. Alloy sheets to be Grade J57S.
2.6. Provide all necessary certification to demonstrate compliance and suitability.
2.7. Source from single manufacturer and single batch.

210 Working procedures 
1. Standard:  To BS 3987 for anodic coatings on wrought aluminium.
